---48:Te69,> 🧠 **Key Concept — IIBF Core Foundation: Asset Financing Structures**
> Equipment leasing and hire purchase enable industrial borrowers to acquire plant, machinery, and commercial vehicles without heavy upfront capital expenditure.

### 🏭 1. Types of Lease Financing

• **Financial Lease (Capital Lease):**
  - A long-term, non-cancellable contract where substantially **all risks and rewards incidental to ownership are transferred to the Lessee**.
  - *Full Payout Lease:* The sum of lease rentals over the primary lease period fully amortizes the purchase cost of the asset plus financing return.
  - Maintenance, insurance, and obsolescence risks are borne by the **Lessee**.
• **Operating Lease:**
  - A short-term, cancellable lease where risks and rewards of ownership **remain with the Lessor**.
  - The lease tenure is significantly shorter than the economic life of the asset (e.g. chartering aircraft, leasing computers or medical devices).
  - Maintenance and servicing are normally borne by the **Lessor**.
• **Leveraged Lease:**
  - A tripartite financing arrangement involving:
    1. *Lessor (Owner/Equity Participant)*: Contributes 20–40% equity.
    2. *Lessee (User)*: Operates the asset.
    3. *Long-term Lenders (Banks/FIs)*: Fund 60–80% of asset cost on a non-recourse basis secured by a mortgage on the asset and assignment of lease rentals.
• **Sale and Leaseback:**
  - A company sells its existing owned operational asset to a leasing company for immediate cash and instantly leases it back to retain uninterrupted operational control while freeing up locked working capital.

### ⚖️ 2. Comprehensive Comparison: Lease Financing vs. Hire Purchase

| Parameter | Lease Financing | Hire Purchase |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Governing Statute** | Indian Contract Act, 1872 / Transfer of Property Act | Hire Purchase Act, 1972 / Sale of Goods Act, 1930 |
| **Ownership of Asset** | Remains with the **Lessor throughout** and after expiry. | Passes to the **Hirer upon full payment of the last installment**. |
| **Depreciation Claim** | Claimed by the **Lessor** in the books. | Claimed by the **Hirer** from the first year itself. |
| **Income Tax Treatment** | Entire lease rental is **100% tax-deductible** as revenue expenditure for the lessee. | Only the **finance charges (interest component)** are tax-deductible; principal is capital outlay. |
| **Initial Down Payment** | Typically **Zero or nominal security deposit**. | Requires substantial upfront margin/down payment (15% to 25%). |
| **Termination Rights** | Financial lease is strictly non-cancellable. | Hirer can terminate before maturity by returning the asset. |

### 📑 3. Modern Accounting Treatment: Ind AS 116 (Leases)

• **Single Lessee Accounting Model:** Replaced Ind AS 17 (and AS 19). Eliminates the distinction between operating and finance leases for the **Lessee**.
• **On-Balance Sheet Recognition:** Lessees must capitalize virtually all leases on their balance sheet by recognizing:
  1. **Right-of-Use (ROU) Asset:** Depreciated over the lease term on a straight-line basis.
  2. **Lease Liability:** Present value of unpaid lease payments discounted at the incremental borrowing rate, amortized with interest expense.

> 🎯 **Top IIBF Traps for Unit 41:**
> 1. **Ownership Transfer in HP:** Ownership does **NOT** transfer on contract signing or down payment; it transfers **only when the last hire installment is fully cleared**.
> 2. **Tax Deductibility of Rentals:** In lease financing, **100% of lease rental payments** are allowed as a deductible business expense, providing a significant tax shield for profit-making corporations.

---

---49:T12dc,> 🧠 **Key Concept — IIBF Core Foundation: Credit Risk Assessment**
> Financial institutions rely on Credit Rating Agencies for debt instrument safety and Credit Information Companies for evaluating retail borrower creditworthiness.

### 🏛️ 1. Credit Rating Agencies (CRAs) — Regulated by SEBI

• **Statutory Framework:** Governed by the **SEBI (Credit Rating Agencies) Regulations, 1999**.
• **Major Indian CRAs:** CRISIL (Credit Rating Information Services of India Ltd, 1987 — first CRA in India), ICRA (1991), CARE Ratings (1993), India Ratings and Research (Fitch group), Infomerics, and Acuité Ratings.
• **Scope:** Rate specific debt instruments (Bonds, Debentures, Commercial Paper, Fixed Deposits) and structured finance products.

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                      LONG-TERM DEBT RATING SCALE CODES                      │
│                                                                             │
│  • AAA   : Highest Safety (Lowest credit risk)                              │
│  • AA    : High Safety                                                      │
│  • A     : Adequate Safety                                                  │
│  • BBB   : Moderate Safety (LOWEST INVESTMENT GRADE BENCHMARK)             │
│  ═════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════  │
│  • BB    : Moderate Risk (SPECULATIVE / JUNK GRADE THRESHOLD)               │
│  • B     : High Risk                                                        │
│  • C     : Very High Risk                                                   │
│  • D     : In Default or expected to default soon                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### 👤 2. Credit Information Companies (CICs) — Regulated by RBI

• **Statutory Framework:** Governed by the **Credit Information Companies (Regulation) Act, 2005 (CICRA 2005)** and RBI guidelines.
• **4 Authorized Operating CICs in India:**
  1. TransUnion CIBIL
  2. Equifax Credit Information Services
  3. Experian Credit Information Company of India
  4. CRIF High Mark Credit Information Services
• **Core Mandate:** Aggregate borrower credit history from banks and NBFCs, and generate individual Credit Information Reports (CIR) and numeric Credit Scores.
• **Numeric Score Spectrum:** Ranges strictly between **300 and 900**.
  - **750 to 900:** Excellent credit profile; qualifies for prime borrower interest rate concessions.
  - **700 to 749:** Good credit profile.
  - **650 to 699:** Average / Moderate risk.
  - **Below 650:** Subprime / High risk of delinquency.
• **Statutory Consumer Rights under RBI Mandates:**
  - Every individual is legally entitled to **one Full Credit Report (FCR) free of cost once every calendar year** from each of the 4 CICs.
  - Member banks must update credit records with CICs on a **fortnightly / monthly basis** (effective 2024: fortnightly reporting).

### ⚖️ 3. Fundamental Distinctions: Rating vs. Scoring

| Parameter | Credit Rating Agencies (CRAs) | Credit Information Companies (CICs) |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Statutory Regulator** | **SEBI** (Securities and Exchange Board of India) | **RBI** (Reserve Bank of India under CICRA 2005) |
| **Target Subject** | Debt securities, commercial paper, corporate entities | Individual retail borrowers and micro/small enterprises |
| **Output Metric** | Qualitative Alphabetical Symbols (`AAA`, `AA`, `BBB-`, `A1+`) | Quantitative 3-Digit Numeric Score (300 to 900) |
| **Evaluation Base** | Macro industry risk, management quality, financial ratios | Historical debt repayment discipline, credit utilization ratio, defaults |
| **Mandatory Rating Requirement** | Mandatory for public issuance of debt securities, CP, CDs | Mandatory for sanctioning retail advances and credit cards |

> 🎯 **Top IIBF Traps for Unit 42:**
> 1. **Regulatory Split:** CRAs are regulated by **SEBI**; CICs are regulated by the **RBI**.
> 2. **Lowest Investment Grade:** **`BBB-`** is the lowest investment-grade rating; **`BB+`** is the beginning of speculative/junk status.
> 3. **Free Credit Report:** All citizens are entitled to **1 free report per year from each of the 4 CICs**.

---4a:T1039,> 🧠 **Key Concept — IIBF Core Foundation: Para-Banking & Non-Core Lines**
> Para-banking refers to non-traditional financial services (bancassurance, mutual fund distribution, PMS, credit cards) undertaken by commercial banks under strict RBI prudential safeguards.

### 🏛️ 1. Permitted Para-Banking Activities & Statutory Norms

• **Bancassurance (Insurance Business):**
  - Commercial banks may undertake insurance distribution purely on a **fee-basis as a Corporate Agent** without taking any financial risk under IRDAI (Registration of Corporate Agents) Regulations.
  - **Open Architecture Norms:** A bank acting as a corporate agent can tie up with up to **9 insurance companies**:
    - Up to **3 Life Insurance** companies.
    - Up to **3 General Insurance** companies.
    - Up to **3 Standalone Health Insurance** companies.
  - *Strict Prohibition on Coercion:* Banks are strictly forbidden from compelling any loan borrower to purchase an insurance policy from their corporate tie-up partner as a condition for loan sanction.
• **Mutual Fund (MF) Distribution:**
  - Banks can distribute mutual fund units on an agency basis without prior RBI approval.
  - *Firewalls:* Services must be purely agency-based; banks cannot guarantee returns, NAV, or invest depositor funds into associated mutual funds.
• **Portfolio Management Services (PMS):**
  - Regulated under SEBI (Portfolio Managers) Regulations, 2020.
  - Commercial banks **CANNOT offer Portfolio Management Services departmentally**. PMS can only be provided through a dedicated subsidiary with prior RBI approval.
• **Credit Card Business:**
  - Scheduled Commercial Banks with a **Net Worth of at least ₹100 Crore** can undertake standalone credit card business independently or in tie-up with card-issuing banks without prior RBI approval.
• **Primary Dealership (PD) Business:**
  - Eligible commercial banks can undertake departmental Primary Dealership in G-Secs with RBI approval if they satisfy:
    1. Minimum Net Worth ≥ **₹1,000 Crore**.
    2. Minimum CRAR ≥ **12.0%**.
    3. Net NPAs not exceeding **3.0%**.
    4. Demonstrated record of net profit over the last 3 consecutive financial years.

### 🛡️ 2. Prudential Caps on Bank Equity Investments in Subsidiaries & Financial Ventures

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    PRUDENTIAL LIMITS ON BANK INVESTMENTS                    │
│                                                                             │
│  • In a Single Financial Services Subsidiary/JV:                            │
│    Max 10% of Bank's Paid-Up Capital and Reserves                           │
│                                                                             │
│  • Aggregate in ALL Subsidiaries, JVs & Financial Ventures:                 │
│    Max 20% of Bank's Paid-Up Capital and Reserves                           │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

• **Mandatory Disclosure in Annual Accounts:**
  - Banks must disclose in the **"Notes to Accounts"** of their annual balance sheet all commissions, fees, and brokerages received from bancassurance, mutual fund distribution, and marketing third-party financial products.

> 🎯 **Top IIBF Traps for Unit 43:**
> 1. **Bancassurance Open Architecture Limits:** Maximum **3 Life + 3 General + 3 Health** insurance companies under corporate agency.
> 2. **Credit Card Net Worth Threshold:** Minimum **₹100 Crore** net worth is mandatory for commercial banks to issue credit cards.
> 3. **Single Subsidiary Investment Cap:** A bank cannot invest more than **10% of its paid-up capital and reserves** in any single financial services subsidiary or joint venture.

---4b:Tcef,Deposit operations and customer protection form the bedrock of statutory banking law in India. Recent legislative overhauls under the Banking Laws (Amendment) Act 2025, DICGC Act, and the Reserve Bank - Integrated Ombudsman Scheme (RB-IOS) 2026 have modernized customer rights, expedited distress payouts, and streamlined estate transmissions.

• Nomination under Banking Laws (Amendment) Act, 2025 (Effective 01.11.2025): Depositors can nominate up to 4 persons per deposit account, safe custody article, or safe deposit locker under either Simultaneous Nomination (with explicit % shares summing to 100%) or Successive Nomination (Nominee 1 -> Nominee 2 -> Nominee 3 -> Nominee 4). For single locker hirers, successive nomination applies; for joint hirers, nomination delivers contents to survivors along with nominee.
• Legal Status of Nominee: A nominee acts as a trustee/custodian for legal heirs, not the absolute beneficial owner (Supreme Court ruling in Sarbati Devi vs Usha Devi). Bank obtains valid discharge under Section 45ZA, but legal heirs retain succession rights.
• RBI Revised Norms for Deposit Accounts of Minors: Minors of any age can open/operate savings & term deposits with natural/legal guardian, with mothers explicitly recognized on par with fathers. Literate minors aged 10+ can independently operate accounts with board-approved velocity limits. Section 11 of Indian Contract Act strictly prohibits overdrafts/credit to minors. Fresh KYC and signature verification mandatory upon turning 18.
• DICGC Deposit Insurance System (DICGC Act 1961): Insures up to ₹5,00,000 per depositor per bank (principal + interest). Covers Commercial Banks, RRBs, Co-op Banks, SFBs, and PBs. Excludes foreign/state/central govt and inter-bank deposits. Account coverage ratio: 97.6%; Value coverage ratio: 41.5%.
• DICGC Section 18A 90-Day Mandatory Interim Payout: When a bank is placed under All-Inclusive Directions (AID) / moratorium by RBI, the bank has 45 days to submit claims, and DICGC has 45 days to verify and disburse up to ₹5 Lakh directly into depositors alternate bank accounts.
• DICGC Risk-Based Premium (RBP) Transition (FY27): Transitioning from flat 12p per ₹100 p.a. to CAMELS-linked risk rating (Tier 1/Tier 2 models offering up to 33.33% risk discount and 25% vintage discount).
• SEBI Revised Nomination Norms for Demat/MFs: Incapacitated single investors can authorize 1 nominee to operate folios. AMC/DP official must conduct physical verification. Strict zero-alteration rule on mobile/email/bank details; redemptions flow exclusively to linked investor bank account.
• Reserve Bank - Integrated Ombudsman Scheme (RB-IOS) 2026 (Effective 01.07.2026): One Nation One Ombudsman architecture covering SCBs, RRBs, SFBs, PBs, NBFCs (>=₹100 Cr assets), and UCBs (>=₹50 Cr deposits). Consequential financial loss compensation increased from ₹20 Lakh to ₹30 Lakh, plus up to ₹1 Lakh for mental harassment/loss of time. Centralised Receipt and Processing Centre (CRPC) at RBI Chandigarh.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Exam Angle → 🔥 HIGH — Pay close attention to statutory classifications, founding years, nodal ministries, and numerical thresholds in 📌 Module 1: Deposit Operations, Customer Rights & Statutory Governance.

---

---4c:T4c3,| Transaction / Activity | Bank's Legal Status | Customer's Legal Status | Governing Statute / Principle |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Deposit of Money in Savings / Current / FD | Debtor (Borrower) | Creditor (Lender) | Common Law of Banking |
| Borrowing via Loan / Cash Credit / Overdraft | Creditor (Lender) | Debtor (Borrower) | Indian Contract Act, 1872 |
| Articles / Valuables in Safe Custody | Bailee | Bailor | Section 148, Indian Contract Act 1872 |
| Hiring Safe Deposit Locker | Lessor (Licensor) | Lessee (Licensee) | Section 45ZE, BR Act / Transfer of Property Act |
| Collection of Cheques, Drafts & Bills | Agent | Principal | Section 182, Indian Contract Act 1872 |
| Pledge of Goods / Securities for a Loan | Pawnee (Pledgee) | Pawnor (Pledgor) | Section 172, Indian Contract Act 1872 |
| Hypothecation of Movable Assets | Hypothecatee | Hypothecator | SARFAESI Act 2002 / Contract Act |
| Mortgage of Immovable Property | Mortgagee | Mortgagor | Section 58, Transfer of Property Act 1882 |
| Issuance of Demand Draft / Pay Order | Debtor | Creditor (Purchaser / Payee) | Negotiable Instruments Act 1881 |
| Money Left for Specific Unexecuted Purpose | Trustee | Beneficiary | Indian Trusts Act 1882 |4d:T63b,Under Indian law and banking conventions, bankers enjoy specific statutory rights to protect their advances and manage operational risks:

• **1. Right of General Lien (Section 171, Indian Contract Act 1872):** A general lien allows a banker to retain any goods, securities, or commercial paper delivered in the ordinary course of business as security for a general balance of account. Unlike a particular lien (which attaches only to the specific goods repaired/handled), a general lien applies across all outstanding obligations unless there is an express contract to the contrary.
• **Exclusions from General Lien:** Safe custody articles, securities left by mistake, money deposited for a specific unfulfilled purpose, and shares deposited for sale through broker.
• **2. Right of Set-Off:** The legal right to merge/combine two accounts of the same customer (one in debit and one in credit) to determine the net liability. Conditions: Debts must be mutual, due and payable immediately, ascertained in sum, and in the *same right and same capacity*.
• **3. Right of Appropriation (Clayton's Rule):** Governed by Sections 59, 60, and 61 of the Indian Contract Act. Under Clayton's Case (1816), in running cash credit/overdraft accounts with a deceased or retired partner/guarantor, payments into the account extinguish the earliest debits first. To preserve guarantor liability, the bank must immediately break the old account and open a new separate account.
• **4. Right to Charge Interest & Incidental Charges:** Permitted under express/implied contract and RBI Prudential Norms.4e:T707,Section 13 of the Banking Companies (Acquisition and Transfer of Undertakings) Act, 1970/1980 mandates strict confidentiality of customer account affairs. Established under Tournier v. National Provincial and Union Bank of England (1924), exceptions exist only under 4 specific heads:

• **1. Disclosure under Compulsion of Law:** Orders under Section 226(3) Income Tax Act, Section 91 CrPC, Section 43C PMLA, Banker's Books Evidence Act 1891, and Foreign Exchange Management Act (FEMA).
• **2. Disclosure in Public Interest:** Prevention of national security threats, terror financing, or major public frauds.
• **3. Disclosure in the Bank's Own Interest:** Filing a legal recovery suit against a defaulting borrower, enforcing SARFAESI, or claiming insurance.
• **4. Disclosure with Customer's Express or Implied Consent:** Providing trade opinions upon written authorization, credit bureau sharing (CIBIL/Experian) under Credit Information Companies (Regulation) Act 2005.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Banker-Customer Relationship:
1. **Locker Relationship vs Safe Custody:** Hiring a locker creates a **Lessor-Lessee** relationship, NOT Bailor-Bailee. The bank does not take possession or knowledge of the locker contents. Safe custody, conversely, is **Bailee-Bailor**.
2. **Right of Set-off Capacity Trap:** A bank CANNOT set off a customer's individual credit balance against a partnership debit balance without specific authorization, nor set off trust accounts against personal liabilities.
3. **Clayton's Rule Operation:** If a partner in a firm dies and the bank continues the running overdraft account without breaking it, subsequent deposits by surviving partners automatically reduce the deceased partner's liability, releasing his estate.

---

---4f:T69b,| Report Type | Statutory Trigger Threshold | Submission Frequency / Deadline | Special Rules |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Cash Transaction Report (CTR) | All individual or integrally connected cash transactions > ₹10 Lakhs in a calendar month | By 15th of the succeeding month | Both deposits and withdrawals in cash are aggregated |
| Suspicious Transaction Report (STR) | Any transaction irrespective of amount giving rise to reasonable suspicion of illicit proceeds/terror financing | Within 7 working days of arriving at conclusion of suspicion | Strict 'Tipping-off' prohibition under Section 12 PMLA (Bank cannot inform customer) |
| Cross Border Wire Transfer Report (CWTR) | All cross-border wire transfers > ₹5 Lakhs or foreign currency equivalent | By 15th of the succeeding month | Both inward and outward wire transfers monitored |
| Non-Profit Organization Transaction Report (NTR) | All receipts by Non-Profit Organizations (NPOs) > ₹10 Lakhs | By 15th of the succeeding month | Applies to registered NPOs/NGOs |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on AML / KYC:
1. **PAN is NOT an OVD for Address:** PAN card serves only as proof of identity (and financial transaction verification), NOT as proof of address since it does not print address details.
2. **STR Threshold Trap:** STR has **NO monetary threshold**. A transaction of even ₹5,000 can trigger an STR if it has no economic rationale or fits money laundering typologies.
3. **Re-KYC Frequency Trap:** High Risk = **2 Years**, Medium Risk = **8 Years**, Low Risk = **10 Years**. Low risk accounts do not require physical branch visits if self-declaration confirms unchanged details.

---50:T686,| Customer Type | Legal Framework / Capacity | Cheque Book / Overdraft Rules | Operational Guardrails |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Minor (Under 10 Years) | Account opened and operated by natural guardian (Father/Mother) or Court Guardian | No overdraft; No personal liability on minor | Guardian signs on behalf of minor. Balance belongs to minor. |
| Minor (10+ Years, Literate) | Permitted by RBI to open and operate self-managed savings/FD account | Cheque book permitted; Overdraft strictly prohibited | Cannot be declared insolvent; Cannot ratify debts upon attaining majority. |
| Illiterate Person | Competent to contract; signs via Left Thumb Impression (LTI) for male / Right Thumb Impression (RTI) for female | Cheque book generally not issued unless with crossed order; cash withdrawal in person only | Thumb impression must be attested by independent witness known to bank. |
| Blind Person | Full legal capacity; equal rights to banking facilities under Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act 2016 | Cheque book, ATM/Debit Card, NetBanking fully permissible with talking ATM aids | Witness required during account opening; no discrimination permitted. |
| Purdahnashin Woman | Strict seclusion; transaction treated with heightened caution by courts | Cheque book permitted; withdrawals via authorized agent or witnessed mandate | Bank must ensure independent legal/financial understanding without undue influence. |
| Insolvent Person | Disqualified from contracting; property vests in Official Assignee / Receiver | Account must be immediately frozen upon receipt of Adjudication Order | Any credit balance must be handed over to the Official Receiver. |51:T6b1,| Parameter | Partnership Firm (Partnership Act 1932) | Hindu Undivided Family (HUF) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Governing Law | Indian Partnership Act, 1932 | Hindu Law (Mitakshara / Dayabhaga) & Hindu Succession Act |
| Creation / Membership | Created by Agreement / Contract between partners | Created by status of birth in the Hindu coparcenary |
| Management Head | All partners have implied authority to bind the firm | Exclusively managed by the **Karta** (eldest coparcener) |
| Liability | Unlimited joint and several liability across all partners | Karta has unlimited liability; Coparceners liable only to extent of their share in joint family property |
| Female Membership | Women can be full partners | Daughters are coparceners by birth post 2005 Amendment |
| Dissolution on Death | Death of partner dissolves firm unless partnership deed specifies otherwise | Death of Karta does not dissolve HUF; next senior coparcener becomes Karta |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Special Customers:
1. **Stopping Payment in Joint Accounts:** In an 'Either or Survivor' account, **ANY** of the joint holders can instruct the bank to stop payment of a cheque drawn by the other holder. But revocation of stop-payment requires consent of **ALL** holders.
2. **Minor as Partner Trap:** Under Section 30 of Indian Partnership Act, a minor CANNOT be a full partner, but can be admitted only to the **benefits of an existing partnership** with consent of all partners. Minor's personal property is not liable.
3. **Delegation by Karta:** A Karta cannot appoint an outsider as agent through Power of Attorney to manage HUF business unless strictly authorized by all adult coparceners.

---52:T50f,| Entity Type | Governing Act | Constitutive Document | Mandatory Account Opening Requirements |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Private Limited Company | Companies Act, 2013 | MoA & AoA + Certificate of Incorporation | Min 2, Max 200 members. Board resolution, KYC of all directors & authorized signatories, CIN, PAN. |
| Public Limited Company | Companies Act, 2013 | MoA & AoA + Certificate of Incorporation | Min 7 members, no upper limit. Board resolution, Certificate of Commencement of Business, PAN, CIN. |
| One Person Company (OPC) | Companies Act, 2013 | MoA & AoA with nominee details | Single member/director who must be an Indian citizen and resident. |
| Section 8 Company | Companies Act, 2013 (Non-profit) | License from Central Govt + MoA/AoA | Formed for charitable/scientific/educational purposes; dividend distribution strictly prohibited. |
| Public / Private Trust | Indian Trusts Act, 1882 (Private) / State Public Trust Acts | Registered Trust Deed | Trust Deed specifying powers of trustees. Trustees must act jointly unless deed explicitly allows single operation. |
| Societies & Clubs | Societies Registration Act, 1860 | Memorandum of Association & Rules/Bylaws | Min 7 members. Registration certificate, list of managing committee members, governing body resolution. |53:T52d,• **Doctrine of Ultra Vires:** Any contract entered into by a company beyond the powers given in its MoA is Ultra Vires (beyond powers) and void ab initio. It cannot be ratified even by 100% shareholder consent.
• **Doctrine of Constructive Notice:** Third parties dealing with a company are presumed to have read and understood its public documents (MoA and AoA registered with RoC).
• **Doctrine of Indoor Management (Turquand's Rule):** Outsiders dealing with a company are entitled to assume that internal procedures and approvals required by AoA have been properly complied with.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Companies and Trusts:
1. **Trustee Delegation Trap:** A trustee **CANNOT delegate** duties or discretionary powers to co-trustees or outsiders unless the Trust Deed expressly permits it (*Delegatus non potest delegare*).
2. **Registration of Charges Deadline:** Standard registration period with RoC is **30 days**. An unregistered charge makes the bank an **unsecured creditor** in the event of company liquidation.
3. **Director's Personal Cheque into Company Account:** A bank should never credit a cheque drawn in favor of a company to the personal account of a director without verified authority; doing so forfeits statutory protection under Section 131 NI Act.

---54:T55e,| Parameter | Garnishee Order (Order 21 Rule 46 CPC) | Income Tax Attachment Order (Sec 226(3) IT Act) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Issuing Authority | Civil Court in execution of decree | Income Tax Officer / Tax Recovery Officer (TRO) |
| Two-Stage Process | Yes: (1) **Order Nisi** (Freeze/Show-cause) followed by (2) **Order Absolute** (Payment direction) | No: Single direct order directing immediate remittance to Govt treasury |
| Application to Future Credits | ❌ **No.** Attaches only funds existing *at the exact moment and date of service* of the order. | ✅ **Yes.** Attaches current credit balance **AND all subsequent future credits** until the full tax demand is cleared. |
| Joint Accounts Rule | Cannot attach joint account (A & B) for individual debt of A unless specified in order | Can attach proportionate share of joint holder (50% presumed unless specified) |
| Priority of Orders | Subordinate to Income Tax Attachment Orders | Enjoys **Statutory Priority** (Crown Debt) over Civil Court Garnishee Orders |
| Effect on Un-cleared Cheques | Does not attach cheques deposited but not yet cleared/credited | Attaches proceeds as soon as they clear into the account |
| Right of Set-off by Bank | Bank can exercise prior right of set-off before calculating attachable balance | Bank can exercise set-off for dues already due and payable before order date |55:T723,Under RBI Master Directions on Customer Service, banks follow structured protocols to settle claims of deceased depositors without causing undue hardship to survivors:

• **1. Accounts with Valid Nomination / Survivorship Mandate:** Claim settled in favor of the nominee or surviving joint holder within **15 days** of receipt of death certificate and KYC proof, without requiring legal representation (Probate, Letters of Administration, or Succession Certificate).
• **2. Accounts without Nomination / Survivorship (Below Threshold Limit):** Banks settle claims based on simple Indemnity Bond, Letter of Disclaimer from other legal heirs, and KYC proof up to board-approved threshold limits (typically ₹1 Lakh to ₹5 Lakhs).
• **3. Accounts with Substantial Balances & Disputes:** Legal representation (Succession Certificate under Indian Succession Act 1925) is mandatory.
• **4. Safe Deposit Lockers of Deceased:** Nominee is allowed to open the locker and inventory the contents in the presence of 2 independent witnesses; nominee receives contents as custodian for legal heirs.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Legal Orders:
1. **Future Deposits in Garnishee Order:** A deposit made in the afternoon *after* a Garnishee Order Nisi was served in the morning is **NOT** attached. The bank must open a separate new account for subsequent transactions.
2. **IT Order Priority:** If both a Garnishee Order and an IT Attachment Order arrive, the **Income Tax Attachment Order takes absolute precedence**.
3. **Deceased Account with Mandate / PoA:** A Mandate or Power of Attorney terminates **immediately** on the death, insanity, or insolvency of the principal. Any cheque signed by a PoA holder presented after the principal's death must be returned with remark 'Drawer Deceased'.

---56:T4a7,| Operational Parameter | RBI Statutory Rule / Standard | Legal Basis / Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Bank's Liability for Locker Incidents | Capped at **100 times the annual locker rent** in case of fire, theft, burglary, dacoity, building collapse, or staff fraud | RBI Circular on Safe Deposit Lockers (2023) |
| Exemption from Liability | Bank not liable for loss arising from natural calamities / Act of God (earthquake, flood) or customer negligence | Common Law of Torts / Contractual terms |
| Term Deposit as Security for Locker | Bank can obtain Term Deposit covering **3 years' rent plus break-open charges** from new hirers | RBI Locker Master Directions |
| Model Locker Agreement | Mandatory revised stamped agreement based on IBA model executed with all hirers | Indian Stamp Act / Contract Act |
| Locker Operation Inactivity Alert | If a locker remains unoperated for **7 years** (even if rent is paid), bank can break open after due notice | RBI Unclaimed Accounts & Lockers Norms |
| Break-Open Protocol | Notice via registered post/email; newspaper publication in 2 dailies; executed in presence of 2 independent witnesses and Videography | SOP on Locker Break-Open |57:T859,Governed by the Banking Companies (Nomination) Rules 1985, nomination provides smooth succession and eliminates probate requirements:

| Form Number | Statutory Purpose | Eligible Facility | Key Legal Provision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Form DA-1 | Registration of Fresh Nomination | Deposit Accounts, Safe Custody, Safe Deposit Lockers | Nomination can be in favor of only ONE individual (except Lockers hired jointly with specific survivorship). |
| Form DA-2 | Cancellation of Existing Nomination | Deposit Accounts, Safe Custody, Lockers | Can be cancelled at any time during depositor's lifetime in writing. |
| Form DA-3 | Variation / Modification of Nomination | Deposit Accounts, Safe Custody, Lockers | Changes nominee name/details without cancelling the facility. |

• **Nominee is a Trustee:** The Supreme Court (Sarojini Ramaswami v. Union of India / Ram Chander Talwar v. Devinder Kumar Talwar) established that the nominee is merely an authorized receiver/trustee for the legal heirs. Nomination does not override the Indian Succession Act.
• **Minor as Nominee:** A minor can be appointed as nominee provided the depositor appoints a major guardian in Form DA-1 to receive funds on the minor's behalf in the event of the depositor's death during minor's minority.
• **Nomination in Partnership / Company Accounts:** Strictly **PROHIBITED**. Nomination is available ONLY to individual depositors (sole or joint).

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Lockers and Nomination:
1. **Locker Compensation Cap:** The maximum compensation a bank is legally liable to pay for locker theft/building collapse is **100 times the annual locker rent** (not the claimed value of gold/jewelry inside).
2. **Nomination for Non-Individuals:** Nomination facility is **NOT available** for Partnership firms, Companies, Trusts, HUFs, or Societies. It applies solely to natural persons.
3. **Multiple Nominees in Deposit Accounts:** Under Section 45ZA, a deposit account can have **ONLY ONE nominee**. Having two nominees (e.g. 50% to son, 50% to daughter) in a single deposit account is legally invalid.

---58:T43e,Under RBI Master Directions on Counterfeit Currency, failure by a bank teller to impound a forged note is treated as a severe compliance failure:

| Parameter | Single Transaction: Up to 4 Forged Notes | Single Transaction: 5 or More Forged Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Impounding Action | Teller stamps 'COUNTERFEIT BANKNOTE IMPOUNDED' and issues signed receipt to presenter | Teller stamps 'COUNTERFEIT BANKNOTE IMPOUNDED' and issues signed receipt to presenter |
| Return of Note to Customer | ❌ **Strictly Prohibited.** No credit/refund given under any circumstances | ❌ **Strictly Prohibited.** No credit/refund given under any circumstances |
| Police Reporting Protocol | Send consolidated report with impounded notes to Nodal Police Station at the **end of the month** | File an **immediate First Information Report (FIR)** with local police station for criminal investigation |
| Preservation of Forged Notes | Maintained in safe custody under joint custodianship until handed over to police | Handed over to Police Investigation Officer against official acknowledgement |59:T411,| Category | Definition / Characteristics | Value Payable (Adjudication Norms) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Soiled Note | A banknote which has become dirty due to normal usage, or a two-piece note pasted together where both pieces belong to the same note and form an entire note without any essential feature missing | Full Value payable across all bank branches free of charge |
| Mutilated Note | A banknote of which a portion is missing or which is composed of more than two pieces pasted together | Adjudicated based on the **surface area of the single largest undivided piece** according to RBI Note Refund Rules |
| Extremely Brittle / Charred / Burnt Notes | Notes severely burnt, charred, or stuck together that cannot be handled without crumbling | Cannot be accepted across bank counters; sent directly to RBI Issue Department for special adjudication |
| Deliberately Cut / Slogans Inscribed | Notes bearing political/religious slogans or intentionally cut/defaced | ❌ **Zero Value.** Rejected and impounded under Note Refund Rules |5a:T4c6,• **For Denominations of ₹50, ₹100, ₹200, ₹500:**.
• • **Full Value:** Single largest undivided piece has an area (ge 80%) of the original note dimensions.
• • **Half Value:** Single largest undivided piece has an area (ge 40%) and (< 80%) of the original note dimensions.
• • **Zero Value:** Single largest undivided piece has an area (< 40%).
• **For Lower Denominations (₹5, ₹10, ₹20):**.
• • **Full Value:** Single largest undivided piece has an area (ge 50%). (No half-value refund for denominations below ₹50).

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Cash Operations:
1. **Police Reporting Slabs for Fake Notes:** Up to 4 notes = **Monthly consolidated report**. 5 or more notes in a single transaction = **Immediate FIR**.
2. **Returning Fake Notes:** A bank employee who returns a counterfeit note to the customer without impounding can be prosecuted under Section 39 of the CrPC for concealing a cognizable offence.
3. **Half-Value Refund Rule:** Half-value refund is available ONLY for denominations of **₹50 and above**. For ₹5, ₹10, and ₹20 notes, there is NO half value (it is either full value if (ge 50%) area or zero value if (< 50%)).

---5b:T10ef,> 🧠 **Key Concept — IIBF Core Foundation: Cheque Operations & Legal Protections**
> The Negotiable Instruments Act, 1881 balances the operational speed of the banking clearing system with statutory protections for paying and collecting bankers against forged instruments.

### 🛡️ 1. Statutory Protections to Paying Banker

• **Section 85(1) (Order Cheque):** Where an order cheque purports to be endorsed by or on behalf of the payee, the paying banker is discharged by **Payment in Due Course**, even if an endorsement turns out to be forged.
• **Section 85(2) (Bearer Cheque):** A cheque originally drawn as a bearer cheque remains payable to bearer regardless of any subsequent endorsements (*"Once a bearer, always a bearer"*). Paying banker is discharged if paid to the bearer in due course.
• **Section 85A (Bank Drafts):** Draft drawn by one branch of a bank upon another branch; paying branch is fully protected if paid in due course despite forged endorsements.
• **Section 128 (Crossed Cheques):** Paying banker is discharged if payment of a crossed cheque is made in good faith and without negligence to a banker (in general crossing) or to the specified banker (in special crossing).

### ✍️ 2. The Absolute Rule on Forged Drawer Signature

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                 FORGED ENDORSEMENT vs. FORGED DRAWER SIGNATURE              │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1. FORGED ENDORSEMENT (Sec 85) : Bank IS PROTECTED if paid in due course.   │
│ 2. FORGED DRAWER SIGNATURE    : Bank IS NOT PROTECTED. Payment is a NULLITY.│
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

• *Legal Reality:* A forged signature of the drawer is completely void (*nullity*). There is no mandate from the customer. The paying banker cannot debit the customer's account, even if the forgery was executed with extreme skill (*Bihta Cooperative Bank vs. Bank of Bihar*).

### 📥 3. Statutory Protection to Collecting Banker (Section 131)

A collecting banker who receives payment of a crossed cheque for a customer incurs no liability to the true owner in case of title defect, provided:
1. The cheque was **crossed before it came into the bank's hands**.
2. The bank collected the cheque **in good faith and without negligence** (proper KYC, verification of apparent alterations).
3. The bank acted purely as an **agent for collection on behalf of a customer** (not as a holder for value).

### ⚖️ 4. Criminal Dishonour of Cheques: Section 138 of NI Act

*Dishonour of a cheque due to insufficiency of funds is a criminal offense punishable with imprisonment up to 2 years, fine up to twice the cheque amount, or both:*

```
  Cheque Presented (Within 3 Months) ──► Returned Dishonoured (Memo Received)
                                                      │
         ┌────────────────────────────────────────────┘
         ▼ (Within 30 Days)
  Demand Notice Issued to Drawer in Writing
         │
         ▼ (Wait 15 Days for Drawer to Pay)
  Drawer Fails to Clear Dues within 15 Days
         │
         ▼ (Within 1 Month)
  Criminal Complaint Filed before Judicial / Metropolitan Magistrate
```

> 🎯 **Top IIBF Traps for Unit 19:**
> 1. **Protection Condition for Collecting Banker:** Protection under Section 131 is available **ONLY if the cheque was crossed prior to deposit**. If an uncrossed cheque is crossed by the bank, Section 131 protection is forfeited.
> 2. **Section 138 Timelines:** Demand notice must be issued within **30 days** of dishonour; drawer is granted **15 days** to pay; complaint must be filed within **1 month** thereafter.

---5c:Tceb,Prudential credit guidelines govern credit risk underwriting, portfolio provisioning, and borrower protections across Scheduled Commercial Banks, NBFCs, and Housing Finance Companies.

• Expected Credit Loss (ECL) Ind-AS 109 / IFRS 9 Framework: Replaces ex-post Incurred Loss Model with ex-ante forward-looking provisioning based on macroeconomic forecasts. Three stages: Stage 1 (Performing, 0-30 DPD) = 12-Month ECL.
• Stage 2 (Underperforming, 31-89 DPD / Significant Increase in Credit Risk - SICR) = Lifetime ECL.
• Stage 3 (Credit-Impaired / Default, 90+ DPD) = Lifetime ECL based on PD x LGD x EAD. 5-year capital transition glide path.
• RBI Co-Lending Model (CLM 1 & CLM 2): Combines low-cost bank capital with last-mile NBFC sourcing reach for Priority Sector. CLM 1 = Joint Lending; CLM 2 = Direct Assignment / Sourcing takeover. Mandatory Invariant: NBFC MUST retain minimum 20% credit risk on its own balance sheet. End-borrower pays blended interest rate. All repayments routed via tripartite Escrow account.
• Fair Practices Code on Penal Charges: Abolished compounding penal interest. Only reasonable, board-approved Penal Charges permitted. Penal charges cannot be capitalized or added to principal balance (no interest charged on penal charges). For individual non-business loans, charges cannot exceed commercial defaults.
• Digital Lending Guidelines 2025 (DLG): Regulated Entities (REs) fully responsible for LSPs/DLAs. Fund disbursals & repayments must flow directly between RE bank account and borrower bank account without synthetic pass-through pool accounts. Key Fact Statement (KFS) disclosing APR mandatory. Minimum cooling-off window: 3 days (tenor >=7 days) or 1 day (<7 days). First Loss Default Guarantee (FLDG) strictly capped at 5% of portfolio.
• Mutual Credit Guarantee Scheme (MCGS) & CGTMSE: Credit guarantee for MSEs up to ₹5 Crore (₹10 Cr for high-tech). Tiered guarantee: 85% for micro loans up to ₹5 Lakh, women, SC/ST, and NER; 75% for standard MSEs.
• Modified Interest Subvention Scheme (MISS): Short-term crop loans up to ₹3 Lakh at benchmark 7.0% p.a. GoI provides 1.5% subvention to banks + 3.0% Prompt Repayment Incentive (PRI) to farmers -> effective net rate of 4.0% p.a.
• PM Vishwakarma Scheme: Collateral-free loans up to ₹3 Lakh (Tranche 1: ₹1L, Tranche 2: ₹2L) at concessional 5.0% interest rate (MoMSME subvention up to 8%).
• Priority Sector Lending (PSL) 2025-26 Overhaul: Housing loan limits categorized into 3 population tiers: >=50 Lakh pop (₹50 Lakh limit), 10L-50L pop (₹45 Lakh limit), <10L pop (₹35 Lakh limit). Urban Co-operative Banks PSL target fixed at 60% ANBC/CEOBE. Renewable Energy PSL limit: ₹35 Crore for utilities, ₹10 Lakh for households.
• Housing Finance Companies (HFCs) Revised Norms: Public deposit ceiling reduced from 3x to 1.5x Net Owned Funds (NOF); liquid assets requirement raised from 13% to 15%; maximum deposit tenor reduced from 10 to 5 years; co-branded credit cards and interest rate hedging permitted.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Exam Angle → 🔥 HIGH — Pay close attention to statutory classifications, founding years, nodal ministries, and numerical thresholds in 📌 Module 2: Credit Risk, Prudential Lending & Fair Lending Frameworks.

---

---5d:T595,$\text{DSCR} = \frac{\text{Net Profit After Tax} + \text{Depreciation} + \text{Interest on Term Loan}}{\text{Interest on Term Loan} + \text{Principal Term Loan Installment}}$

*Explanation:* Measures borrower's ability to service annual term debt obligations. Benchmark: Average DSCR should ideally be between 1.50 and 2.00. Minimum acceptable DSCR is generally 1.20.

$\text{BEP (\% of capacity)} = \frac{\text{Fixed Costs}}{\text{Installed Capacity Output} \times (\text{Selling Price} - \text{Variable Cost per unit})} \times 100$

*Explanation:* Indicates the level of production/sales where Total Revenue equals Total Cost (Zero Profit, Zero Loss). Lower BEP indicates higher margin of safety for lenders.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Working Capital & Term Loans:
1. **Tandon Method 2 Formula Trap:** Method 2 margin is 25% of **Total Current Assets**, NOT 25% of Working Capital Gap. Formula is: \(\text{MPBF} = (0.75 \times CA) - CL\).
2. **Current Ratio Benchmark:** Method 1 gives a Current Ratio of **1.17:1**, whereas Method 2 mandates a minimum Current Ratio of **1.33:1**.
3. **Nayak Committee Parameters:** Nayak Committee applies to credit limits up to **₹5 Crores** for MSEs. Projected annual turnover \(T\) is assessed; Total Working Capital Requirement = \(25\% \text{ of } T\), of which Bank Finance = \(20\% \text{ of } T\) and Borrower Margin = \(5\% \text{ of } T\).

---5e:T92c,| Mortgage Type | Section in TP Act | Creation Method & Formalities | Bank's Remedy / Sale Power without Court |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Equitable Mortgage (Deposit of Title Deeds) | Section 58(f) | Delivery of original title deeds to the bank with intent to create security in notified towns. No stamp duty/registration needed in most states. | Enforceable via SARFAESI Act 2002 without court intervention; or via civil court decree for sale. |
| Simple Mortgage | Section 58(b) | Registered mortgage deed without delivery of possession. Mortgagor binds himself personally to pay the mortgage money. | Bank has no right to sell property without obtaining a Civil Court decree. |
| Mortgage by Conditional Sale | Section 58(c) | Ostensible sale of property with condition that sale becomes void on repayment, or becomes absolute on default. | Remedy is **Foreclosure** (court order debarring mortgagor from redeeming), NOT sale. |
| Usufructuary Mortgage | Section 58(d) | Delivery of possession to mortgagee who receives rents and profits in lieu of interest/principal. | Mortgagee cannot sue for sale or foreclosure; recovers debt strictly from property usufruct (rents/yields). |
| English Mortgage | Section 58(e) | Absolute transfer of property to mortgagee with agreement to re-transfer upon repayment on a fixed date. | Mortgagee has power to sell property without court intervention under Section 69 TP Act in specific conditions. |
| Anomalous Mortgage | Section 58(g) | A mortgage which is not any of the above 5 types (e.g. combination of simple and usufructuary mortgage). | Terms governed strictly by the custom and express terms in the mortgage deed. |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Collaterals & Charges:
1. **Pledge vs Hypothecation Possession:** In a Pledge, possession is **ALWAYS with the Bank**. In Hypothecation, possession is **ALWAYS with the Borrower**.
2. **Equitable Mortgage Requirements:** 3 essential requisites for a valid Equitable Mortgage under Sec 58(f): (a) a debt, (b) deposit of **ORIGINAL title deeds**, and (c) execution in a town notified by the State Government.
3. **Foreclosure Remedy:** The remedy of **Foreclosure** is available ONLY in *Mortgage by Conditional Sale* and certain *Anomalous Mortgages*. It is **NEVER** available in Simple or Equitable mortgages.

---5f:T4ad,| Party | Role & Description | Primary Statutory Obligation |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Applicant (Opener) | The Buyer / Importer who requests his bank to open the LC | Reimburses the Issuing Bank upon presentation of compliant documents. |
| Issuing Bank (Opening Bank) | The Buyer's bank that issues the LC and gives the primary payment undertaking | Under primary, irrevocable liability to pay beneficiary upon presentation of compliant documents. |
| Advising Bank | Bank in seller's country that authenticates and communicates the LC to the beneficiary | Responsible only for verifying the apparent authenticity of the credit. |
| Confirming Bank | A bank that adds its own independent payment undertaking to the LC at issuing bank's request | Assumes same irrevocable liabilities as the Issuing Bank (dual bank guarantee for seller). |
| Negotiating / Nominated Bank | The bank authorized to examine documents and pay / advance funds to the beneficiary | Examines documents under UCPDC 600 and negotiates compliant drafts. |
| Beneficiary | The Seller / Exporter in whose favor the credit is issued | Entitled to receive payment upon presenting strictly compliant documents within LC validity. |60:T5f9,| LC Type | Operational Mechanism | Exam Significance / Commercial Purpose |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Irrevocable LC | Cannot be amended or cancelled without the consent of all parties (Issuing Bank, Confirming Bank, Beneficiary) | Under UCPDC 600, all LCs are **presumed Irrevocable** unless stated otherwise. |
| Confirmed LC | Confirming bank adds its independent undertaking to pay | Protects seller against Issuing Bank default and country/political risk. |
| Transferable LC | Can be transferred in whole or part by the 1st Beneficiary to 2nd Beneficiary(ies) | Used by middleman/trading agents. Can be transferred **only ONCE** unless stated. |
| Back-to-Back LC | A second separate LC opened by seller's bank on the security of the master incoming LC | Used when the master LC is non-transferable. |
| Red Clause LC | Contains an express clause permitting the advising bank to grant **pre-shipment clean advance** to the seller | Provides working capital to exporter before manufacturing/shipping goods. |
| Green Clause LC | Permits pre-shipment advance for **warehousing and storage** at port of shipment in addition to manufacturing | Requires warehouse receipts as collateral. |
| Standby LC (SBLC) | Operates as a financial guarantee; payable only upon default/non-performance of applicant | Governed by UCPDC 600 or ISP98 (International Standby Practices). |
| Revolving LC | Amount is automatically reinstated after utilization without requiring fresh amendments | Used for regular, repetitive commercial shipments. |61:T43f,• **Article 14(b) - 5 Banking Days Rule:** The Issuing Bank, Confirming Bank, or Nominated Bank each has a maximum of **5 banking days** following the day of receipt of documents to determine whether the presentation is compliant.
• **Article 16 - Notice of Refusal:** If documents are non-compliant, the bank must give a single notice of refusal stating all discrepancies by telecommunication (SWIFT MT734) before the close of the 5th banking day. Failure to do so precludes the bank from claiming non-compliance.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Letters of Credit:
1. **Document Examination Timeline:** Under UCPDC 600, the maximum time to examine documents is **5 banking days**, NOT 7 days (7 days was the old UCP 500 rule).
2. **Presumption of Irrevocability:** Under UCPDC 600 Article 3, an LC is **ALWAYS irrevocable** even if it does not state so on its face.
3. **Red Clause vs Green Clause:** Red Clause covers **pre-shipment manufacturing advance**. Green Clause covers **pre-shipment advance PLUS storage/warehousing at port of origin**.

---

---62:T406,| Guarantee Type | Purpose / Commercial Use Case | Bank Risk Profile & Margin Guidelines |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Financial Guarantee | Guarantees repayment of a financial debt (e.g. advance payment guarantee, loan repayment, customs/excise duty payment) | High financial risk. RBI mandates higher cash margin / tangible collateral. |
| Performance Guarantee | Guarantees timely completion of a commercial contract, project construction, or service delivery | Relatively lower financial risk; bank must evaluate applicant's technical competence and past execution track record. |
| Deferred Payment Guarantee (DPG) | Guarantees payment of machinery/equipment purchases in installments over a medium-to-long-term horizon | Treated as a Term Loan equivalent; requires full financial appraisal and DSCR evaluation. |
| Bid Bond / Earnest Money Guarantee | Guarantees that bidder will not withdraw tender and will execute contract if awarded | Short-term validity (typically 3–6 months); converts to Performance Guarantee on award. |63:T62c,A Bank Guarantee is an independent contract between the Bank and the Beneficiary. The bank must honor invocation on demand without inquiring into the underlying dispute between buyer and seller:

• **Rule of Absolute Independence:** The bank is not concerned with whether the principal debtor has committed breach or whether the claim is justified, provided invocation strictly complies with guarantee terms.
• **Court Injunction Exceptions:** Courts grant injunctions against BG invocation ONLY under 2 exceptional circumstances (established in *U.P. State Sugar Corp v. Sumac International Ltd*): (1) **Established Fraud of egregious nature** known to the bank, or (2) **Irretrievable Injustice / Irreparable Harm**.
• **Section 28 of Indian Contract Act (Amendment):** Guarantees can stipulate a specific claim period. If the guarantee provides for a claim period, it must allow a **minimum claim period of 1 year** beyond the guarantee expiry date for enforcing rights against the bank.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Bank Guarantees:
1. **Restraint on Invocation:** Banks **CANNOT refuse** payment on an unconditional bank guarantee merely because the customer (principal debtor) disputes the claim or requests stop-payment.
2. **Section 28 Claim Period Rule:** Under Section 28 of ICA, any clause curtailing the claim period to less than **1 year** from the expiry date is legally void.
3. **DPG Nature:** Deferred Payment Guarantee is evaluated on **Term Loan Appraisal principles** because installments mature over multiple years.

---64:T5c5,ECGC provides credit insurance covers to banks and direct exporters to mitigate risks associated with cross-border trade:

• **1. Packing Credit Guarantee (WTPCG):** Protects banks against losses due to insolvency or default of the exporter in repaying pre-shipment advances.
• **2. Post-Shipment Export Credit Guarantee (WTPSG):** Protects banks against non-realization of export bills discounted/purchased.
• **3. Standard Policy to Exporters:** Protects exporters against **Commercial Risks** (insolvency of buyer, protracted default, refusal to accept goods) and **Political Risks** (war, civil strife, import restrictions, exchange transfer blockage by foreign government).
• **Risks NOT Covered by ECGC:** Quality disputes, buyer's non-payment due to exporter's fault, foreign exchange fluctuations.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Export Finance:
1. **Liquidation of Packing Credit:** Packing credit **CANNOT be repaid with local cash/funds**. It MUST be liquidated from export proceeds or by converting into post-shipment bills.
2. **Export Proceeds Realization Deadline:** Under FEMA 1999 regulations, export proceeds must be realized within **9 months** from the date of shipment (15 months for warehouses established abroad).
3. **Gold Card Scheme:** Valid for **3 years** (renewable), issued to exporters with good track record of 3 years, offering 20% standby limits and faster processing within 25 days for fresh limits.

---65:T4f1,| Section in SARFAESI | Enforcement Stage | Statutory Timelines & Powers | Borrower / Guarantor Rights |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Section 13(2) | Demand Notice to Borrower | Mandatory **60-day notice** issued to discharge liabilities in full after account is classified as NPA. | Borrower can submit representation/objection within 60 days. |
| Section 13(3A) | Disposal of Objections | Bank must reply to borrower's representation within **15 days** of receipt. | Rejection of objection gives no immediate right to appeal in DRT. |
| Section 13(4) | Enforcement / Possession Measures | Upon failure to pay after 60 days, bank takes symbolic/physical possession of secured asset, takes over management, or appoints manager. | Borrower can appeal to DRT within **45 days** under Section 17. |
| Section 14 | Magistrate Assistance | Chief Metropolitan Magistrate (CMM) or District Magistrate (DM) takes physical possession of asset and hands over to bank. | Administrative act; Magistrate cannot adjudicate dispute on merits. |
| Section 17 | Appeal to DRT (Securitisation Application) | Borrower files application against Sec 13(4) measures within 45 days. | Appeal to Appellate Tribunal (DRAT) under Sec 18 requires **50% pre-deposit** (can be reduced to 25%). |66:T507,| Recovery Forum | Governing Act | Monetary Claim Jurisdiction | Nature of Order |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Lok Adalat | Legal Services Authorities Act, 1987 | Up to **₹20 Lakhs** (for bank compromise settlements) | Consent decree; No appeal lies against award |
| Debt Recovery Tribunal (DRT) | RDB Act, 1993 (Recovery of Debts and Bankruptcy) | **₹20 Lakhs and above** | Issues Recovery Certificate; Appeal to DRAT within 30 days |
| Insolvency & Bankruptcy Code (IBC) | Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016 (NCLT) | Default threshold of **₹1 Crore and above** | CIRP process: Resolution within 180 + 90 days (max 330 days) |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on NPA Management & SARFAESI:
1. **SARFAESI Agricultural Land Exemption:** Under Section 31(i), the SARFAESI Act does **NOT apply to agricultural land**.
2. **SARFAESI Debt Thresholds:** SARFAESI cannot be invoked if: (a) remaining debt is less than **₹1 Lakh**, or (b) remaining principal and interest is less than **20% of the original principal debt**.
3. **DRAT Pre-Deposit Requirement:** To appeal before the Debt Recovery Appellate Tribunal (DRAT) under Section 18 of SARFAESI, the borrower must deposit **50% of the debt determined by DRT** (DRAT can reduce it to minimum 25%).

---67:Tc8b,> 🧠 **Key Concept — IIBF Core Foundation: Working Capital Assessment & TAT Redressal**
> Commercial credit appraisal determines working capital limits using standardized regulatory methods, while electronic banking enforces statutory compensation for failed payment turnaround times.

### 🏭 1. Working Capital Assessment Methodologies

#### A. Turnover Method (Nayak Committee Recommendations for MSMEs)
• *Applicability:* For working capital loan limits up to **₹5 Crore** for MSMEs and small businesses.
• **Total Working Capital Requirement:** Estimated at **25% of projected annual turnover**.
• **Promoter Margin (Borrower Contribution):** Minimum **5% of projected turnover**.
• **Maximum Bank Finance:** **20% of projected turnover**.

$$\text{Bank Working Capital Limit} = \text{Projected Annual Turnover} \times 20\%$$

#### B. Tandon Committee: Maximum Permissible Bank Finance (MPBF)
*Working Capital Gap (WCG) = Current Assets (CA) - Current Liabilities other than Bank Borrowings (CL)*

| Method | Bank Finance Share | Borrower Long-Term Margin Share | Minimum Current Ratio Benchmark |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Method 1** | $$\text{MPBF}_1 = 0.75 \times (\text{CA} - \text{CL})$$ | 25% of Working Capital Gap (WCG) | **1.17 : 1** |
| **Method 2** | $$\text{MPBF}_2 = (0.75 \times \text{CA}) - \text{CL}$$ | 25% of total Current Assets (CA) | **1.33 : 1** |

### 📈 2. Term Loan Viability: Debt Service Coverage Ratio (DSCR)

$$\text{DSCR} = \frac{\text{Net Profit After Tax} + \text{Depreciation} + \text{Annual Term Loan Interest}}{\text{Annual Principal Installment} + \text{Annual Term Loan Interest}}$$
• **Ideal Industry Benchmark:** Between **1.5 : 1 and 2.0 : 1**.
• **Minimum Acceptable Ratio:** **1.25 : 1**. A DSCR below 1.0 indicates that company cash flows are insufficient to service debt without external infusion.

### ⏱️ 3. RBI Harmonisation of Turn Around Time (TAT) & Penalty Compensation

*Under RBI's circular, if a customer's account is debited but the transaction fails, the bank must reverse the funds within the prescribed TAT or pay mandatory auto-compensation without requiring customer complaint:*

| Payment Channel | Authorized Resolution TAT | Mandatory Penalty for Delay |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **ATM / Cash Recycler** (Cash not dispensed) | **T + 5 calendar days** | **₹100 per day** of delay |
| **Point of Sale (PoS) / E-Commerce** | **T + 5 calendar days** | **₹100 per day** of delay |
| **UPI Transactions** (Beneficiary not credited) | **T + 1 calendar day** | **₹100 per day** of delay |
| **IMPS (Instant Interbank Transfer)** | **T + 1 calendar day** | **₹100 per day** of delay |
| **NACH / Direct Debit Reversals** | **T + 1 calendar day** | **₹100 per day** of delay |

> 🎯 **Top IIBF Traps for Unit 20:**
> 1. **Nayak Method Percentages:** Total WC requirement = **25%**; Bank finance = **20%**; Borrower margin = **5%** of projected annual turnover.
> 2. **Tandon Method 2 Current Ratio:** Requires minimum Current Ratio of **1.33:1**.
> 3. **Compensation Auto-Credit:** The penalty of **₹100/day** must be credited automatically to the customer's account without requiring any formal claim.

---68:T9dc,India's Digital Public Infrastructure (DPI) and fintech ecosystem have driven massive shifts in payment velocity, data protection, cyber fraud defense, and open banking models.

• Unified Payments Interface (UPI) Advanced Modes: UPI Lite (On-device wallet, ₹2,000 max balance, ₹500 per-txn cap, zero PIN, near-zero failure rate).
• UPI 123PAY (4 non-internet modes: IVR numbers, Missed call, Sound-based proximity, and OEM apps).
• UPI Circle (delegated primary-secondary user authorization).
• Credit Lines on UPI (pre-approved bank credit lines accessed via UPI QR).
• BHIM 3.0: 22-language AI voice assistant, automated family spend management, biometric authentication, real-time integration with Centralised Fraud Registry.
• Finternet (BIS Unified Ledger Model): Conceptualized by Agustin Carstens & Nandan Nilekani. The Three U's: User-centric (user retains asset control), Unified (connects multiple asset classes on programmable smart contracts), Universal (interoperable across borders).
• Digital Personal Data Protection (DPDP) Rules 2025: Data Protection Board of India (DPBI). SARAL approach (Simple, Accessible, Rational, Actionable notices). Maximum penalties: ₹250 Crore for security safeguards failure preventing data breach; ₹200 Crore for non-notification of breach or child data violations. 90-day grievance redressal mandate.
• Financial Fraud Risk Indicator (FRI): Joint DoT & I4C initiative integrating Chakshu platform and NCRP portal. Scores suspect mobile numbers as Medium, High, or Very High Risk in real time during digital onboarding and KYC.
• Banking as a Service (BaaS) & Embedded Finance: Licensed banks expose CBS APIs for white-label debit cards, embedded BNPL checkout, corporate payroll, and digital escrow.
• Stablecoins & Crypto Assets: Pegged to fiat/commodities. Global laws: EU MiCA (100% liquid reserve backing), US GENIUS Act, China total ban. India: Cryptos not legal tender; RBI highlights currency substitution, seigniorage loss, and deposit flight. Digital Rupee (CBDC e₹-R & e₹-W) issued as sovereign alternative.
• EASE 8.0 PSB Reforms: 4 Themes: Risk & Resilience, Innovation (GenAI), Excellence (CASA growth & cost optimization), Socio-Economic Impact (inclusive lending).

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Exam Angle → 🔥 HIGH — Pay close attention to statutory classifications, founding years, nodal ministries, and numerical thresholds in 📌 Module 4: Digital Banking, Payments Architecture & Cyber Governance.

---69:T595,| System / Metric | Technical Definition | Regulatory / Industry Benchmark | Operational Role in Banking |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Primary Data Center (PDC) | The central facility housing core database servers, storage arrays, and network gateways | Tier III or Tier IV Data Center standard with 99.98%–99.99% uptime | Hosts live production environment for all branch and digital channels. |
| Disaster Recovery Site (DRS) | Geographically separated secondary data center located in a different seismic zone | Minimum distance of 200–500 km from PDC in a different natural risk zone | Takes over live banking operations seamlessly if PDC suffers fire, earthquake, or catastrophic failure. |
| Recovery Point Objective (RPO) | The maximum acceptable duration of data loss measured in time preceding a disaster | **Near-Zero RPO (Real-time synchronous data replication)** | Ensures zero committed customer transactions are lost during failover. |
| Recovery Time Objective (RTO) | The maximum acceptable duration required to restore systems and resume banking services post-disaster | **RTO typically < 30 to 60 Minutes** | Minimizes financial downtime and maintains payment system stability. |
| Disaster Recovery Drills | Live simulation switching production load from PDC to DRS | Mandatory periodic drills (at least **twice a year / half-yearly**) | Validates operational readiness of backup systems and connectivity. |6a:T5bd,• **1. Deposit Module:** Automated interest accrual, TDS calculation at financial year-end, auto-renewal of term deposits, and dormant account flags.
• **2. Loans & Advances Module:** Asset classification into standard/NPA based on 90-day overdue logic, interest charging on daily reducing balances, and automated installment billing.
• **3. General Ledger (GL) & Financial Reporting:** Real-time generation of Daily Balance Sheet and Profit & Loss statement without manual end-of-day branch tallying.
• **4. Trade Finance & Forex Module:** Integrated processing of Letters of Credit, Bank Guarantees, Forward Contracts, and SWIFT messaging (MT103, MT700).
• **5. Delivery Channel Integration:** Real-time API middleware connecting CBS with ATM Switch, POS networks, Mobile Banking, Internet Banking, and UPI gateways.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Bank Computerization & CBS:
1. **RPO vs RTO Distinction:** **RPO (Recovery Point Objective)** measures **data loss** in time. **RTO (Recovery Time Objective)** measures **system downtime** until restoration.
2. **UCIC Mandate:** Under RBI norms, a customer must have only **ONE unique CIF/UCIC** in the entire bank, even if they hold multiple accounts across different branches in India.
3. **DRS Location Norm:** The Disaster Recovery Site must be located in a **different seismic/geographical zone** to ensure both centers cannot be disabled by the same natural disaster.

---6b:T43a,| Parameter | RTGS (RBI) | NEFT (RBI) | IMPS (NPCI) | UPI (NPCI) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Settlement Mode | Gross Settlement in Real-Time | Deferred Net Settlement (DNS) in half-hourly batches | Real-Time Gross Immediate Credit | Real-Time Instant Credit via Virtual Payment Address (VPA) |
| Operating Hours | **24x7x365** (Round-the-clock) | **24x7x365** (48 half-hourly batches daily) | **24x7x365** (Instant real-time) | **24x7x365** (Instant real-time) |
| Minimum Amount | **₹2,00,000 (₹2 Lakhs)** | No Minimum (Re. 1) | No Minimum (Re. 1) | No Minimum (Re. 1) |
| Maximum Limit | No Maximum Ceiling | No Maximum Ceiling | **₹5,00,000 (₹5 Lakhs)** per transaction | **₹1,00,000** standard (₹5L for Hospitals/Educational/Tax payments) |

• **Two Versions:**.
• • **e₹-W (Wholesale):** Launched Nov 2022 for interbank settlement of secondary market transactions in Government Securities.
• • **e₹-R (Retail):** Launched Dec 2022 as token-based digital currency in denominations identical to physical currency notes and coins, held in non-interest-bearing digital wallets.
• **Zero Interest Bearing:** Unlike bank deposits, CBDC does not earn interest to prevent sudden disintermediation of commercial bank deposits.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Payment Systems:
1. **RTGS Minimum Amount Limit:** The minimum limit for an RTGS transaction is **₹2,00,000**. There is NO minimum limit for NEFT.
2. **IMPS Maximum Transaction Limit:** The maximum transaction limit for IMPS is **₹5,00,000 (₹5 Lakhs)** (revised from ₹2 Lakhs in 2021).
3. **UPI Standard Limit:** Standard UPI limit is **₹1 Lakh** per transaction (extended to ₹5 Lakhs for tax payments, hospitals, and educational institutions).
4. **CBDC Interest Trap:** Digital Rupee (e₹) carries **ZERO interest** (same as physical cash in hand).

---6d:T4d4,| Security Dimension | Mandatory RBI Guideline / Standard | Operational Enforcement Mechanism |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Security Operations Centre (SOC) | Mandatory **24x7x365 SOC** equipped with SIEM (Security Information & Event Management) tools | Real-time threat monitoring, anomaly detection, and automated log analysis. |
| Mandatory Incident Reporting | All unusual cyber incidents, data breaches, or ransomware attacks must be reported to RBI and **CERT-In within 6 Hours** | Submission of initial cyber incident report within 6 hours followed by root-cause analysis. |
| Two-Factor Authentication (2FA) | Mandatory Additional Factor of Authentication (AFA) for all card-not-present and digital fund transfers | OTP, Hardware Token, or Biometric verification alongside static password. |
| Network Segmentation | Separation of database servers, application servers, and external internet-facing web servers with firewalls | Demilitarized Zone (DMZ) architecture preventing direct external access to core databases. |
| VAPT (Vulnerability Assessment & Penetration Testing) | Mandatory periodic VAPT audits of all internet-facing banking applications | Conducted at least **twice a year** by CERT-In empaneled security auditors. |6e:T6bf,| Section in IT Act | Offence / Statutory Mandate | Penalties / Legal Consequence |

| Section 43A | Failure of a corporate entity / bank to protect sensitive personal data resulting in wrongful loss | Liable to pay damages by way of compensation to the affected person (no upper cap). |
| Section 66 | Hacking / Computer related offences with fraudulent intention | Imprisonment up to **3 years** or fine up to **₹5 Lakhs** or both. |
| Section 66C | Identity Theft (fraudulent use of electronic signature, password, or unique identification feature) | Imprisonment up to **3 years** and fine up to **₹1 Lakh**. |
| Section 66D | Cheating by Personation using computer resource / mobile phone (Phishing / Vishing frauds) | Imprisonment up to **3 years** and fine up to **₹1 Lakh**. |
| Section 66F | Cyber Terrorism (acts aimed at threatening unity, integrity, security of India or disabling critical infrastructure) | Imprisonment for **LIFE**. |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Cyber Security & IT Act:
1. **CERT-In Reporting Deadline:** Cyber security incidents must be reported to CERT-In within **6 HOURS** of detection (not 24 hours or 48 hours).
2. **Cyber Terrorism Penalty:** The maximum penalty under Section 66F for cyber terrorism targeting critical financial/national infrastructure is **Life Imprisonment**.
3. **ISO 27001 Standard:** ISO 27001 is the standard for **Information Security Management Systems (ISMS)**, while ISO 9001 is for General Quality Management.

---6f:T513,| Customer Right | Statutory Objective | Operational Application in Bank Branches |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Right to Fair Treatment | Customers cannot be discriminated against on grounds of gender, age, religion, caste, or physical disability | Equal access to all banking facilities, transparent loan pricing, and uniform service terms. |
| 2. Right to Transparency, Fair & Honest Dealing | Contracts, fee schedules, interest rates, and penalties must be transparent, clear, and easily understandable | Key Fact Statement (KFS) provided for loans; no hidden charges or unfair lock-in clauses. |
| 3. Right to Suitability | Products offered to customers must match their financial needs, risk appetite, and capacity | Strict prohibition on **Mis-selling** of insurance, mutual funds, or derivative products to naive/senior citizen depositors. |
| 4. Right to Privacy | Customer's personal and financial information must remain strictly confidential | Data cannot be shared with telemarketers or third parties without explicit affirmative consent. |
| 5. Right to Grievance Redressal & Compensation | Right to prompt dispute resolution and fair compensation for bank's service deficiencies | Time-bound resolution by Principal Nodal Officer; escalation to **RBI Integrated Ombudsman Scheme 2021**. |70:T6c8,• **1. Basel Corporate Governance Guidelines for Banks:** Mandates strong board oversight, clear risk appetite statements, independent Risk Management and Audit Committees, and separation of Chairman and CEO roles.
• **2. Whistleblower Policy (Section 177, Companies Act 2013):** Every listed company and scheduled bank must establish a vigil mechanism allowing employees and directors to report unethical behavior, actual or suspected fraud, or violation of the bank's code of conduct with **adequate safeguards against victimisation**.
• **3. Chinese Wall Doctrine:** An ethical and physical barrier established within financial institutions to prevent the flow of sensitive, non-public information between different departments (e.g. between Investment Banking/M&A advisory and Treasury/Stock Broking units) to eliminate **Insider Trading** and conflicts of interest.
• **4. Prevention of Mis-Selling:** Mandatory cooling-off / free-look period of **15 to 30 days** in retail insurance and investment products allowing customers to cancel policies without penalty.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Banking Ethics & Governance:
1. **RBI Charter of Customer Rights Count:** The RBI Charter contains exactly **5 Core Rights** (Fair Treatment, Transparency, Suitability, Privacy, and Grievance Redressal).
2. **Mis-Selling and Right to Suitability:** Selling a high-risk ULIP or equity fund to an illiterate rural depositor seeking a fixed deposit is a direct statutory violation of the **Right to Suitability**.
3. **Chinese Wall Principle:** The Chinese Wall specifically exists to prevent **conflict of interest and insider trading** between investment advisory/M&A and trading/treasury divisions.

---71:T8de,| Accounting Concept | Core Theoretical Definition | Practical Accounting Application in Banking / Business |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Business Entity Concept | Business and its owners are distinct separate entities | Owner's drawings are debited to Drawing Account; Capital is shown as a liability on the Balance Sheet. |
| Money Measurement Concept | Only transactions quantifiable in monetary terms are recorded | Employee skill, brand loyalty, or staff health are not recorded in accounts. |
| Going Concern Concept | Business will continue operating indefinitely without liquidation intent | Fixed assets are recorded at historical cost less depreciation, not at current liquidation value. |
| Accounting Period Concept | Business life is divided into discrete time intervals (usually 1 year, 1 Apr to 31 Mar) | Mandates annual preparation of Profit & Loss Account and Balance Sheet. |
| Cost / Historical Cost Concept | Assets are recorded at the original acquisition purchase price | Market fluctuations in land value do not alter balance sheet asset value until realized. |
| Dual Aspect Concept | Every transaction affects at least two accounts with equal Debit and Credit | Fundamental Accounting Equation: \(\text{Assets} = \text{Liabilities} + \text{Capital}\). |
| Accrual Concept | Revenue recognized when earned; Expense recognized when incurred | Interest earned on loans is credited as income even if not yet collected in cash. |
| Realization / Revenue Recognition | Revenue recognized when legal title/ownership of goods passes to buyer | Advance payment received from customer is a liability until goods are delivered. |
| Matching Concept | Revenues of a period must be matched with expenses incurred to generate that revenue | Depreciation on machinery used to manufacture goods is charged against the same year's sales revenue. |
| Conservatism / Prudence Concept | Anticipate no profit, but provide for all possible losses | Closing stock valued at **Cost or Net Realizable Value (Market Price), whichever is LOWER**; Provision for Bad Debts. |
| Materiality Concept | Items with significant economic impact must be disclosed separately | Insignificant items (e.g. pencil purchase) charged immediately to expense rather than capitalized as assets. |72:T491,• **Ind AS Framework:** Converged with International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS) issued by the Ministry of Corporate Affairs (MCA) under Section 133 of the Companies Act 2013.
• **Key Ind AS for Banks:**.
• • **Ind AS 109 (Financial Instruments):** Mandates **Expected Credit Loss (ECL)** model replacing the traditional incurred loss model for loan loss provisioning.
• • **Ind AS 115 (Revenue from Contracts with Customers):** Standardized 5-step model for revenue recognition.
• • **Ind AS 116 (Leases):** Requires recognition of Right-of-Use (RoU) assets and lease liabilities on the balance sheet for operating leases.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Accounting Concepts:
1. **Valuation of Closing Stock:** Valuing closing stock at *Cost or Market Price, whichever is LOWER* is an application of the **Prudence / Conservatism Concept**.
2. **Capital as a Liability:** Capital is shown as a liability on the Balance Sheet due to the **Business Entity Concept**.
3. **Accrual vs Cash Basis:** Recording accrued interest and outstanding rent at year-end is mandated by the **Accrual and Matching Concepts**.
