---73:T4b3,• **Step 1: Source Document:** Transaction occurs supported by voucher, invoice, or receipt.
• **Step 2: Journalizing (Book of Original Entry):** Chronological recording of transactions with debit and credit legs accompanied by a brief explanation (*Narration*).
• **Step 3: Ledger Posting (Principal Book of Accounts):** Transferring debit and credit entries from Journal into individual T-shaped ledger accounts.
• **Step 4: Balancing Ledger Accounts:** Calculating the difference between total debit side and total credit side at month/year end to establish the closing balance (Debit Balance if Debit > Credit; Credit Balance if Credit > Debit).

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Golden Rules:
1. **Outstanding Rent Classification:** 'Outstanding Rent A/c' is a **Representative Personal Account**, NOT a Nominal Account. (Rent is Nominal, but Outstanding Rent represents liability to landlords).
2. **Bank Account Classification:** A Bank Account (e.g. SBI Account) is a **Personal Account** (Artificial Legal Person), NOT a Real Account.
3. **Luca Pacioli Origin:** Double-entry bookkeeping was formulated by Italian mathematician **Luca Pacioli in 1494**.

---

---74:T7cb,| Error Category | Nature / Mechanism | Impact on Trial Balance Tally | Rectification Method |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Error of Principle | Transaction recorded in violation of fundamental accounting principles (e.g. Capital expenditure treated as Revenue expenditure) | ❌ **Trial Balance Tallies** (Does NOT affect total debits/credits) | Debit correct Asset A/c; Credit wrong Expense A/c |
| Error of Complete Omission | A transaction is completely omitted from being recorded in Journal and Ledger | ❌ **Trial Balance Tallies** | Pass original journal entry in full |
| Compensating Error | Two or more independent errors cancel each other out in terms of monetary effect | ❌ **Trial Balance Tallies** | Rectify individual erroneous ledger accounts |
| Error of Commission (Posting to Wrong Account) | Amount posted to wrong account of the same class (e.g. Debited to Shyam instead of Ram) | ❌ **Trial Balance Tallies** | Debit correct person; Credit wrong person |
| Error of Partial Omission | Transaction entered in Journal but posted only to one ledger leg | 🚨 **Trial Balance Fails to Tally** | Rectified via Suspense Account |
| Error in Casting / Under-casting | Wrong mathematical totaling of a subsidiary book or ledger account | 🚨 **Trial Balance Fails to Tally** | Rectified via Suspense Account |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Trial Balance & Errors:
1. **Capital Expenditure Charged to Revenue:** Treating installation charges of new machinery as 'Repairs' is an **Error of Principle**. It will **NOT** affect the agreement of the Trial Balance.
2. **Suspense Account Nature:** Suspense Account is a temporary account. If it has a debit balance at final accounts stage, it appears on the **Assets side** of Balance Sheet; if credit balance, on the **Liabilities side**.
3. **Compensating Errors:** When Ram's account is over-debited by ₹500 and Shyam's account is under-debited by ₹500, they are **Compensating Errors**.

---

---75:T5ec,| Transaction / Cause of Discrepancy | Cash Book Impact | Pass Book Impact | Action when Starting from Cash Book (+ / −) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Cheques issued to suppliers but not yet presented for payment | Deducted (−) | Not yet deducted (Higher) | **ADD (+)** to Cash Book |
| Cheques deposited into bank but not yet cleared / collected | Added (+) | Not yet credited (Lower) | **SUBTRACT (−)** from Cash Book |
| Interest credited directly by bank in Passbook | Not yet recorded (Lower) | Added (+) | **ADD (+)** to Cash Book |
| Direct payment made by bank under standing instructions (Insurance/Rent) | Not yet recorded (Higher) | Deducted (−) | **SUBTRACT (−)** from Cash Book |
| Bank charges and commission debited by bank | Not yet recorded (Higher) | Deducted (−) | **SUBTRACT (−)** from Cash Book |
| Direct deposit by customer into bank account | Not yet recorded (Lower) | Added (+) | **ADD (+)** to Cash Book |
| Dishonor of cheque discounted / deposited | Recorded as cash in hand | Debited (−) | **SUBTRACT (−)** from Cash Book |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on BRS:
1. **Passbook Debit Balance:** A **Debit balance in Bank Passbook** indicates an **OVERDRAFT** (unfavorable balance for customer).
2. **Cash Book Credit Balance:** A **Credit balance in Cash Book** indicates an **OVERDRAFT**.
3. **Cheques Issued but Not Presented:** When starting with a favorable Cash Book balance, cheques issued but not presented must be **ADDED (+)**.

---

---76:T4d1,| Parameter | Straight Line Method (SLM) | Written Down Value Method (WDV) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Calculation Basis | Calculated on **Original Cost** every year | Calculated on **Written Down Book Value** of that year |
| Annual Depreciation Amount | Constant / Fixed every year | Declines progressively every year |
| Asset Book Value at End of Life | Becomes exactly Equal to Scrap Value (or Zero) | Never reaches Zero |
| Income Tax Act Recognition | Recognized only for Power Generating units | Mandatorily recognized for all business classes under Income Tax Act 1961 |
| Combined Impact (Depreciation + Repairs) | Unfair impact: Total burden increases in later years as repairs rise | Balanced impact: Falling depreciation offsets rising repair costs |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Depreciation:
1. **Depreciation on Land:** Land is **NEVER depreciated** under accounting standards because its useful life is unlimited.
2. **Income Tax Act Requirement:** For tax computation under the Income Tax Act 1961, **WDV method is mandatorily used** across blocks of assets.
3. **WDV Zero Book Value Trap:** Under WDV method, the book value of an asset can **never mathematically reach absolute zero**.

---77:T64d,$\text{PV} = \frac{\text{FV}}{(1 + r)^n} = \text{FV} \times (1 + r)^{-n}$

*Explanation:* Where \(PV\) = Present Value, \(FV\) = Future Value, \(r\) = interest rate per compounding period, and \(n\) = total number of compounding periods.

$\text{PV}_{\text{Annuity}} = C \times \left[ \frac{1 - (1 + r)^{-n}}{r} \right]$

*Explanation:* Used to determine the maximum loan amount sanctionable for a given equated periodic installment \(C\).

$\text{PV}_{\text{Perpetuity}} = \frac{C}{r}$

*Explanation:* A perpetuity is a stream of equal cash flows \(C\) that continues indefinitely forever (e.g. British Consols, irredeemable preference shares).

$\text{PV}_{\text{Growing Perpetuity}} = \frac{C}{r - g} \quad (r > g)$

*Explanation:* Where cash flows grow at constant annual rate \(g\) indefinitely (provided discount rate \(r > g\)).

$\text{EAR} = \left( 1 + \frac{r_{\text{nominal}}}{m} \right)^m - 1$

*Explanation:* Reflects true annual yield when interest is compounded \(m\) times per year (e.g. quarterly compounding in bank deposits where \(m=4\)).

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on TVM & Annuity:
1. **Annuity Due vs Ordinary Annuity:** \(\text{Value of Annuity Due} = \text{Value of Ordinary Annuity} \times (1 + r)\). Annuity Due is always higher by a factor of \((1+r)\) because payments earn interest for one extra period.
2. **Nominal Rate vs Effective Rate:** If nominal rate is 12% p.a. compounded monthly, \(\text{EAR} = (1 + 0.01)^{12} - 1 = 12.68\%\), which is higher than 12%.
3. **Perpetuity Formula:** Present value of perpetuity is simply \(\frac{C}{r}\).

---

---78:T14d1,> 🧠 **Key Concept — IIBF Core Foundation: Statutory Bank Balance Sheet & P&L**
> Under Section 29 of the Banking Regulation Act, 1949, commercial banks must prepare their final accounts strictly in accordance with the 16 statutory schedules of Form A (Balance Sheet) and Form B (Profit & Loss) prescribed in the Third Schedule.

### 🏛️ 1. Form A: Balance Sheet Architecture (Schedules 1 to 12)

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                 FORM A: BALANCE SHEET STATUTORY SCHEDULES                   │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ CAPITAL & LIABILITIES:                                                      │
│   • Schedule 1  : Capital (Authorised, Issued, Subscribed, Paid-Up)         │
│   • Schedule 2  : Reserves & Surplus (Statutory, Capital, Share Premium)    │
│   • Schedule 3  : Deposits (Demand, Savings Bank, Term Deposits)            │
│   • Schedule 4  : Borrowings (In India from RBI/Banks, Outside India)       │
│   • Schedule 5  : Other Liabilities & Provisions (Bills Payable, Accrued)   │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ ASSETS:                                                                     │
│   • Schedule 6  : Cash and Balances with Reserve Bank of India              │
│   • Schedule 7  : Balances with Banks & Money at Call and Short Notice      │
│   • Schedule 8  : Investments (G-Secs, Shares, Bonds, Subsidiaries)         │
│   • Schedule 9  : Advances (Bills Discounted, Cash Credits, Term Loans)     │
│   • Schedule 10 : Fixed Assets (Premises, Furniture & Fixtures)             │
│   • Schedule 11 : Other Assets (Inter-office adjustments, Non-banking assets)│
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ OFF-BALANCE SHEET FOOTNOTE:                                                 │
│   • Schedule 12 : Contingent Liabilities (Letters of Credit, Bank Guarantees)│
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

### 📈 2. Form B: Profit & Loss Account Architecture (Schedules 13 to 16)

| Section | Schedule Code & Name | Sub-Components Included |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **I. Income** | **Schedule 13: Interest Earned** | • Interest/discount on advances and bills.<br>• Income on investments.<br>• Interest on balances with RBI and inter-bank funds. |
| | **Schedule 14: Other Income** | • Commission, exchange, and brokerage fees.<br>• Net profit on sale of investments.<br>• Net profit on exchange transactions.<br>• Dividends from subsidiaries. |
| **II. Expenditure** | **Schedule 15: Interest Expended** | • Interest paid on deposits (Savings, Term deposits).<br>• Interest on RBI and inter-bank borrowings. |
| | **Schedule 16: Operating Expenses** | • Employee salaries, provisions, and allowances.<br>• Rent, taxes, lighting, printing, stationery.<br>• Depreciation on bank premises and assets.<br>• Director fees, auditor fees, legal charges. |
| | *Provisions & Contingencies* | *(Direct Line in P&L, Not a Schedule)*: NPA provisions, standard asset provisioning, income tax provision. |

### ⚖️ 3. Critical Statutory Reserve & Disclosure Rules

• **Mandatory Transfer to Statutory Reserve (Section 17):** Every banking company must transfer **at least 20% of its net profit** (RBI recommends and enforces **25%** for commercial banks) each year before declaring any dividend.
• **Non-Banking Assets Acquired in Satisfaction of Claims (Section 9):** Real estate or physical goods taken over from defaulted borrowers must be shown under **Schedule 11 (Other Assets)** and legally disposed of within **7 years** (RBI can extend up to an additional 5 years).
• **Bills for Collection:** Cheques and bills received for collection are **NOT entered into the Balance Sheet balance**; they are disclosed purely as an informational footnote below Schedule 12.

> 🎯 **Top IIBF Traps for Unit 11:**
> 1. **LCs and Guarantees:** Appear in **Schedule 12 (Contingent Liabilities)**, outside the balance sheet total.
> 2. **Bills Payable:** Falls under **Schedule 5 (Other Liabilities and Provisions)**; Bills Purchased/Discounted fall under **Schedule 9 (Advances)**.
> 3. **Statutory Reserve Transfer:** Minimum statutory requirement is **20% under Sec 17(1) BR Act**, while the operational RBI benchmark is **25%**.

---79:Tcaf,> 🧠 **Key Concept — IIBF Core Foundation: Forex Rates & Merchant Pricing**
> Foreign exchange transactions involve buying and selling one national currency against another. Understanding direct quotation, two-way pricing, bid-ask spreads, and cross rates is mandatory for banking treasury operations.

### 🌐 1. Quotation Systems: Direct vs. Indirect Quotes

• **Direct Quotation (Home Currency Price of Foreign Unit):**
  - Expresses fixed 1 unit of foreign currency in variable units of domestic currency (e.g. `1 USD = INR 84.50`).
  - *Indian Market Practice:* Effective **August 2, 1993**, India shifted from indirect to **Direct Quotation** for all merchant and interbank dealings.
  - *Trading Golden Rule:* **Buy Low, Sell High** (Bank buys foreign currency cheaper and sells it at a higher rate).
• **Indirect Quotation (Foreign Currency Price of Domestic Unit):**
  - Expresses fixed units of home currency in variable units of foreign currency (e.g. `INR 100 = USD 1.18`).
  - *Trading Golden Rule:* **Buy High, Sell Low**.

### 🧮 2. Bid-Ask Spread & Two-Way Quotations

Banks quote two rates simultaneously:
$$\text{USD / INR} = 84.20 / 84.30$$
• **Bid Rate (Buying Rate):** ₹84.20 (Rate at which the bank buys foreign currency).
• **Ask Rate (Offer / Selling Rate):** ₹84.30 (Rate at which the bank sells foreign currency).
• **Spread Formula:**
  $$\text{Bid-Ask Spread} = \text{Ask Rate} - \text{Bid Rate} = 84.30 - 84.20 = \text{₹}0.10$$
  $$\text{Spread Percentage} = \frac{\text{Ask Rate} - \text{Bid Rate}}{\text{Ask Rate}} \times 100$$

### 🔗 3. Cross Rate Calculation (Chain Rule)

*When a direct rate between two currencies is not quoted in the local interbank market, the rate is derived through a vehicle currency (usually USD):*

$$\text{EUR / INR} = (\text{USD / INR}) \times (\text{EUR / USD})$$
*Example:* If $\text{USD / INR} = 84.00$ and $\text{EUR / USD} = 1.08$:
$$\text{EUR / INR} = 84.00 \times 1.08 = \text{₹}90.72$$

### 📅 4. Forward Rates & Forward Margin Rules

$$\text{Forward Rate} = \text{Spot Rate} \pm \text{Forward Margin (Premium or Discount)}$$

| Forward Margin Pattern | Market State | Operational Rule in Direct Quotation |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Ascending Order** (e.g. `0.20 / 0.30`) | **Foreign Currency at Premium** | **ADD** margins to Spot Bid and Spot Ask. |
| **Descending Order** (e.g. `0.35 / 0.25`) | **Foreign Currency at Discount** | **SUBTRACT** margins from Spot Bid and Spot Ask. |

• **Merchant Card Rates Classification:**
  1. *TT Buying Rate:* Inward telegraphic remittances where funds are already credited to bank's Nostro account.
  2. *Bills Buying Rate:* Purchase/discount of foreign export documentary bills (involves transit interest + credit risk).
  3. *TT Selling Rate:* Outward clean remittances, issue of foreign demand drafts.
  4. *Bills Selling Rate:* Payment of foreign import bills.

> 🎯 **Top IIBF Traps for Unit 12:**
> 1. **Forward Rule:** In direct quote: **Ascending points = Premium (ADD)**; **Descending points = Discount (SUBTRACT)**.
> 2. **Bank Principle:** In buying foreign currency, the bank quotes the lower rate (gives fewer Rupees); in selling, the bank quotes the higher rate (takes more Rupees).

---7a:T6f0,| Technique | Considers TVM? | Calculation Formula / Decision Rule | Key Strength & Limitation |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Payback Period (PBP) | ❌ No | Time required to recover initial investment: \(\frac{\text{Initial Outlay}}{\text{Annual Cash Inflow}}\) | Simple liquidity measure; ignores cash flows after payback period. |
| Accounting Rate of Return (ARR) | ❌ No | \(\frac{\text{Average Annual Profit after Tax}}{\text{Average Investment}} \times 100\) | Uses accounting profit instead of cash flows; ignores TVM. |
| Net Present Value (NPV) | ✅ Yes | \(\text{NPV} = \sum_{t=1}^n \frac{C_t}{(1+k)^t} - C_0\). Accept if \(\text{NPV} > 0\) | Theoretically superior; assumes cash flows reinvested at Cost of Capital (\(k\)). |
| Internal Rate of Return (IRR) | ✅ Yes | Discount rate \(r\) where \(\text{NPV} = 0\). Accept if \(\text{IRR} > k\) | Popular with corporate boards; assumes reinvestment at IRR rate (unrealistic). |
| Profitability Index (PI / Benefit-Cost) | ✅ Yes | \(\text{PI} = \frac{\text{Present Value of Inflows}}{\text{Initial Investment}}\). Accept if \(\text{PI} > 1.0\) | Best for capital rationing with constrained investment budgets. |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Capital Budgeting:
1. **NPV vs IRR Conflict:** When evaluating mutually exclusive projects where NPV and IRR give conflicting rankings, **NPV method must ALWAYS be preferred** because it maximizes shareholder wealth.
2. **Cash Flows vs Accounting Profit:** In Capital Budgeting, we evaluate **Cash Inflows (Profit After Tax + Depreciation)**, NOT accounting profits.
3. **Reinvestment Rate Assumption:** NPV assumes cash flows are reinvested at the **Cost of Capital (\(k\))**, whereas IRR assumes reinvestment at the **IRR itself**.

---7b:T800,| Ratio Category | Formula | Banking Benchmark / Target | Significance for Lenders |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Current Ratio (CR) | \(\frac{\text{Current Assets (CA)}}{\text{Current Liabilities (CL)}}\) | **1.33 : 1** (Tandon Method 2) | Measures short-term solvency and working capital cushion. |
| Quick / Acid-Test Ratio | \(\frac{\text{Current Assets} - \text{Inventories} - \text{Prepaid Expenses}}{\text{Current Liabilities}}\) | **1.00 : 1** | Instant liquidity test excluding slow-moving inventory. |
| Debt-Equity Ratio (DER) | \(\frac{\text{Total Long-Term Debt}}{\text{Tangible Net Worth (Net Owned Funds)}}\) | **2 : 1** (or lower; 3:1 for infra projects) | Measures capital structure leverage and financial cushion of equity holders. |
| Interest Coverage Ratio (ICR) | \(\frac{\text{EBIT (Earnings Before Interest & Tax)}}{\text{Interest Expense}}\) | **\ge 2.00\text{ times}** | Measures ability to pay annual interest obligations from operating profits. |
| Debt Service Coverage Ratio (DSCR) | \(\frac{\text{PAT} + \text{Depreciation} + \text{Interest}}{\text{Interest} + \text{Principal Installment}}\) | **1.50 to 2.00** (Min 1.20) | Primary benchmark for sanctioning term loans. |
| Inventory Turnover Ratio (ITR) | \(\frac{\text{Cost of Goods Sold (COGS)}}{\text{Average Inventory}}\) | Higher indicates fast-moving stock | Detects inventory hoarding or obsolete stock build-up. |
| Debtors Collection Period | \(\frac{\text{Average Accounts Receivable}}{\text{Net Credit Sales}} \times 365\) | Within credit terms (e.g. 60–90 days) | Measures speed of realizing cash from credit customers. |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Ratio Analysis:
1. **Quick Ratio Exclusion:** When calculating Quick Assets, **Inventories and Prepaid Expenses are SUBTRACTED** from Current Assets.
2. **Tandon Committee Benchmark:** The standard minimum Current Ratio under Tandon Method 2 is **1.33:1**.
3. **Ideal Debt-Equity Ratio:** Standard acceptable commercial benchmark for Debt-Equity Ratio is **2:1**.

---7c:Ta4c,> 🧠 **Key Concept — IIBF Core Foundation: Financial Structuring & Leverage**
> Corporate finance evaluates the cost of debt versus equity and measures the risk magnification generated by fixed operating costs and financial obligations.

### 💰 1. Component Cost of Capital & WACC

• **Cost of Debt ($K_d$):**
  Interest payments on debt are tax-deductible, creating an effective corporate tax shield:
  $$K_d = I \times (1 - t)$$
  *(where $I$ = Interest rate on debt, $t$ = Corporate income tax rate)*.
• **Cost of Equity ($K_e$ via Capital Asset Pricing Model — CAPM):**
  $$K_e = R_f + \beta \times (R_m - R_f)$$
  *(where $R_f$ = Risk-free rate, $\beta$ = Beta coefficient of systematic risk, $R_m$ = Expected return of market portfolio, $(R_m - R_f)$ = Equity risk premium)*.
• **Weighted Average Cost of Capital (WACC / $K_o$):**
  $$\text{WACC} = \left(W_d \times K_d\right) + \left(W_e \times K_e\right) + \left(W_p \times K_p\right)$$
  *(where $W$ represents the proportion of each financing component in the capital structure)*.

### ⚙️ 2. The Three Dimensions of Leverage

```
  Sales Revenue ──(less Variable Costs)──► Contribution
         │
         ├── [Operating Leverage: Fixed Operating Costs]
         ▼
  EBIT (Earnings Before Interest & Taxes)
         │
         ├── [Financial Leverage: Fixed Financial Interest Charges]
         ▼
  EBT (Earnings Before Taxes) ──► Net Income ──► Earnings Per Share (EPS)
```

| Leverage Type | Mathematical Formula | Measures & Signifies |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Operating Leverage (OL)** | $$\text{OL} = \frac{\text{Contribution}}{\text{EBIT}}$$ | **Business Risk**: Magnification of EBIT resulting from changes in sales volume due to fixed operating costs. |
| **Financial Leverage (FL)** | $$\text{FL} = \frac{\text{EBIT}}{\text{EBT}} = \frac{\text{EBIT}}{\text{EBIT} - \text{Interest}}$$ | **Financial Risk**: Magnification of EPS resulting from changes in operating profit due to fixed debt interest charges. |
| **Combined Leverage (CL)** | $$\text{CL} = \text{OL} \times \text{FL} = \frac{\text{Contribution}}{\text{EBT}}$$ | **Total Risk**: Total percentage change in EPS for a given percentage change in sales revenue. |

> 🎯 **Top IIBF Traps for Unit 13:**
> 1. **Tax Shield on Dividends:** Interest on debt is **tax-deductible**; dividend payments on equity and preference shares are **NOT tax-deductible**.
> 2. **Zero Leverage Threshold:** If a firm has zero fixed operating costs, its **Operating Leverage is 1.0** (not 0). If a firm has no debt interest, its **Financial Leverage is 1.0**.

---7d:T4a4,$\text{P/V Ratio} = \frac{\text{Contribution}}{\text{Sales}} \times 100 = \frac{\text{Sales} - \text{Variable Cost}}{\text{Sales}} \times 100$

*Explanation:* Expresses profitability per unit of sales volume. Higher P/V ratio indicates high profit-earning capacity.

$\text{BEP (Units)} = \frac{\text{Fixed Cost}}{\text{Contribution per Unit}}, \quad \text{BEP (Value)} = \frac{\text{Fixed Cost}}{\text{P/V Ratio}}$

*Explanation:* The sales volume at which Total Revenue equals Total Cost (Profit = 0).

$\text{Margin of Safety (MoS)} = \text{Actual Sales} - \text{Break-Even Sales} = \frac{\text{Profit}}{\text{P/V Ratio}}$

*Explanation:* The safety cushion by which sales can decline before the business begins incurring losses.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Marginal Costing:
1. **Fixed Cost per Unit Trap:** Total Fixed Cost remains constant, but **Fixed Cost per unit DECREASES as production volume increases**.
2. **Variable Cost per Unit Trap:** Variable cost per unit remains **CONSTANT**, but total variable cost increases proportionally with volume.
3. **Margin of Safety Formula:** \(\text{MOS} = \frac{\text{Profit}}{\text{P/V Ratio}}\).

---7e:T508,| GST Component | Levying Authority | Applicability / Transaction Nature | Input Tax Credit (ITC) Rules |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| CGST (Central GST) | Central Government | Intra-State supply of goods and services | Can be utilized against CGST and IGST |
| SGST (State GST) / UTGST | State Government / Union Territory | Intra-State supply of goods and services | Can be utilized against SGST and IGST |
| IGST (Integrated GST) | Central Government (shared with States) | Inter-State supplies and Imports into India | Can be utilized against IGST, CGST, and SGST |
| GST Council | Article 279A of Constitution | Chaired by Union Finance Minister; decisions taken by **75% weighted majority** | Determines tax slabs (0%, 5%, 12%, 18%, 28%) and banking exemptions |

> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Taxation & TDS:
1. **TDS Threshold for Senior Citizens:** Under Section 194A / 80TTB, the TDS threshold for Senior Citizens (60+ years) is **₹50,000**, compared to **₹40,000** for general citizens.
2. **TDS Rate without PAN:** If a depositor fails to submit PAN, TDS must be deducted at **20%** under Section 206AA.
3. **Form 15G vs 15H:** **Form 15G** is for individuals below 60 years. **Form 15H** is strictly for Senior Citizens (60 years and above).

1. **Risk Concentration in Retail:** Retail banking has **LOW credit concentration risk** because advances are distributed across a massive base of individual borrowers.
2. **SBU Model:** The Strategic Business Unit (SBU) approach creates autonomous profit centers with dedicated operational heads for retail, corporate, and treasury divisions.
3. **CASA Deposits Significance:** Retail deposits (CASA) provide **low-cost, sticky funds** that reduce interest rate sensitivity compared to volatile wholesale bulk deposits.

---80:T5e6,| Product | Loan / Credit Slabs | Margin Requirement | Security / Collateral Mandates |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Education Loan (Studies in India / Abroad) | Up to **₹4.00 Lakhs** | **NIL (0% Margin)** | Co-obligation of parents. **NO third-party guarantee or collateral security** can be demanded. |

1. **Housing Loan LTV Caps:** Maximum LTV for loans up to ₹30L is **90%**; for loans > ₹75L it is **75%**.
2. **Collateral for Education Loans:** For education loans up to **₹4 Lakhs**, demanding collateral security or third-party guarantee is a direct violation of RBI/IBA guidelines.
3. **External Benchmark Lending Rate (EBLR):** Since October 1, 2019, all new floating rate retail loans (housing, auto, personal) must be mandatorily linked to an **External Benchmark** (e.g. RBI Policy Repo Rate).

---81:T56e,| Dimension | Mandatory Regulatory Guideline / Standard | Legal Authority / Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DRA Certification | All recovery agents must undergo 50/100-hour training and hold mandatory **IIBF DRA Certification** | RBI Master Directions on Recovery Agents |

2. **CIBIL Score Range:** CIBIL scores range from **300 to 900 points**.
3. **CIBIL Score Factor Weightages:** **Past Payment History (35%)** has the highest individual weightage in determining a borrower's credit score.

---82:T12f6,> 🧠 **Key Concept — IIBF Core Foundation: Retail Prudential Norms & Wealth Laws**
> Retail banking requires strict compliance with RBI prudential Loan-to-Value (LTV) limits, IBA model education financing norms, service marketing delivery, and RERA buyer protections.

### 🏠 1. Regulatory Loan-to-Value (LTV) Ratios for Housing Loans

*To prevent systemic asset bubbles, the RBI mandates loan ceilings linked to property market valuations:*

| Individual Housing Loan Category | Maximum Permissible LTV Ratio | Minimum Borrower Margin |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Up to ₹30 Lakh** | **90%** | **10%** |
| **Above ₹30 Lakh to ₹75 Lakh** | **80%** | **20%** |
| **Above ₹75 Lakh** | **75%** | **25%** |

• *LTV Calculation Rule:* For loans above ₹10 Lakh, stamp duty, registration charges, and documentation expenses **cannot be included** in the total property cost for calculating the LTV ratio.

### 🎓 2. IBA Model Education Loan Scheme Norms

| Loan Quantum Slab | Margin Requirement | Collateral & Security Mandate |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Up to ₹4.00 Lakh** | **Nil (0% Margin)** | **No Collateral, No Third-Party Guarantee**. Parents/guardians as co-borrowers. |
| **₹4.00 Lakh to ₹7.50 Lakh** | • **5%** for studies in India<br>• **15%** for studies abroad | Third-party guarantee acceptable to bank; **No tangible collateral**. |
| **Above ₹7.50 Lakh** | • **5%** for studies in India<br>• **15%** for studies abroad | **Tangible Collateral Security** of full value + co-obligation of parents. |

• **Moratorium / Repayment Holiday:** Course duration + **1 year** (or 6 months after securing employment, whichever is earlier).
• **Interest Rate Concession:** 1% interest concession if simple interest is serviced during the study and moratorium period.

### 📢 3. Extended Marketing Mix for Banking Services (The 7 Ps)

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                 THE 7 Ps OF FINANCIAL SERVICES MARKETING                    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1. PRODUCT           : Savings deposits, personal loans, credit cards.      │
│ 2. PRICE             : Interest rate spread, processing fees, penal charges.│
│ 3. PLACE             : Physical branches, ATMs, mobile banking apps, BCs.   │
│ 4. PROMOTION         : Digital marketing, branding, financial literacy.     │
│ 5. PEOPLE            : Branch staff, tellers, certified relationship mgrs.  │
│ 6. PROCESS           : Instant digital onboarding, e-KYC, turnaround times. │
│ 7. PHYSICAL EVIDENCE : Modern branch architecture, secure UI/UX, passbooks. │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

• **Cross-Selling vs. Up-Selling in Retail Banking:**
  - *Cross-Selling:* Selling an ancillary, complementary financial product to an existing customer (e.g. selling motor insurance or mutual fund SIP to a home loan borrower).
  - *Up-Selling:* Persuading a customer to upgrade to a higher-tier product (e.g. upgrading a savings account to a premium wealth account with higher minimum balance).

### 🏢 4. Real Estate Regulation Act (RERA, 2016) Safeguards

• **Mandatory Project Registration:** Every residential or commercial project with land area exceeding **500 square meters** or containing more than **8 apartments** must be registered with State RERA prior to public advertising or sale.
• **Mandatory 70% Escrow Account:** Promoters must deposit **70% of the funds realized from allottees into a dedicated scheduled bank escrow account** to cover land and construction costs. Funds can only be withdrawn based on percentage completion certified by an engineer, architect, and chartered accountant.
• **Structural Defect Guarantee:** Promoter must rectify any structural defects or poor workmanship within **5 years** of handover without charging the buyer.

> 🎯 **Top IIBF Traps for Unit 21:**
> 1. **LTV Cap on Large Loans:** Loans above ₹75 Lakh have a maximum LTV of **75%** (minimum 25% margin).
> 2. **Education Loan Collateral:** Collateral cannot be demanded for loans **up to ₹7.50 Lakh** under the IBA model scheme.
> 3. **RERA Escrow Proportion:** Exactly **70% of buyer collections** must be locked into the dedicated project escrow account.

---83:T5b8,| Segment | Annual Income / Wealth Range | Primary Products & Delivery Channel |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Mass Banking | Annual Income up to ₹10 Lakhs | Basic savings accounts, PMJDY, micro-loans, digital channels (UPI, Mobile Banking) |
| Mass Affluent | Annual Income ₹10 Lakhs to ₹50 Lakhs | Housing loans, Auto loans, Credit cards, Term deposits, Mutual fund SIPs |
| Super Affluent / Emerging HNWIs | Annual Income ₹50 Lakhs to ₹2 Crores | Wealth management, Priority banking lounges, customized investment portfolios |
| High Net Worth Individuals (HNWIs) | Investible Surplus ₹2 Crores to ₹25 Crores | Dedicated Private Banking, Portfolio Management Services (PMS), Estate Planning |
| Ultra HNWIs | Investible Surplus > ₹25 Crores | Family Office services, Alternate Investment Funds (AIFs), Global asset diversification |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Retail Banking Characteristics:
1. **Risk Concentration in Retail:** Retail banking has **LOW credit concentration risk** because advances are distributed across a massive base of individual borrowers.
2. **SBU Model:** The Strategic Business Unit (SBU) approach creates autonomous profit centers with dedicated operational heads for retail, corporate, and treasury divisions.
3. **CASA Deposits Significance:** Retail deposits (CASA) provide **low-cost, sticky funds** that reduce interest rate sensitivity compared to volatile wholesale bulk deposits.

---84:T12f6,> 🧠 **Key Concept — IIBF Core Foundation: Retail Prudential Norms & Wealth Laws**
> Retail banking requires strict compliance with RBI prudential Loan-to-Value (LTV) limits, IBA model education financing norms, service marketing delivery, and RERA buyer protections.

### 🏠 1. Regulatory Loan-to-Value (LTV) Ratios for Housing Loans

*To prevent systemic asset bubbles, the RBI mandates loan ceilings linked to property market valuations:*

| Individual Housing Loan Category | Maximum Permissible LTV Ratio | Minimum Borrower Margin |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Up to ₹30 Lakh** | **90%** | **10%** |
| **Above ₹30 Lakh to ₹75 Lakh** | **80%** | **20%** |
| **Above ₹75 Lakh** | **75%** | **25%** |

• *LTV Calculation Rule:* For loans above ₹10 Lakh, stamp duty, registration charges, and documentation expenses **cannot be included** in the total property cost for calculating the LTV ratio.

### 🎓 2. IBA Model Education Loan Scheme Norms

| Loan Quantum Slab | Margin Requirement | Collateral & Security Mandate |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Up to ₹4.00 Lakh** | **Nil (0% Margin)** | **No Collateral, No Third-Party Guarantee**. Parents/guardians as co-borrowers. |
| **₹4.00 Lakh to ₹7.50 Lakh** | • **5%** for studies in India<br>• **15%** for studies abroad | Third-party guarantee acceptable to bank; **No tangible collateral**. |
| **Above ₹7.50 Lakh** | • **5%** for studies in India<br>• **15%** for studies abroad | **Tangible Collateral Security** of full value + co-obligation of parents. |

• **Moratorium / Repayment Holiday:** Course duration + **1 year** (or 6 months after securing employment, whichever is earlier).
• **Interest Rate Concession:** 1% interest concession if simple interest is serviced during the study and moratorium period.

### 🏛️ 1. Master Chronology: Evolution of Indian Banking (1770–Present)

| Era / Milestone | Bank / Institution | Founding Year & Statutory Basis | Historical Significance & Exam Trap |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **First Modern Bank** | Bank of Hindustan | 1770 (Calcutta, Alexander & Co.) | First Western-style joint-stock bank in India; liquidated in 1832. |
| **Early Pioneer** | General Bank of India | 1786 | Second bank established; liquidated in 1791. |
| **Presidency Banks** | Bank of Calcutta (later Bengal) | 1806 (Chartered 1809) | First Presidency Bank. Issued own currency notes before 1861 Paper Currency Act. |
| | Bank of Bombay | 1840 | Second Presidency Bank. |
| | Bank of Madras | 1843 | Third Presidency Bank (amalgamation of Madras Bank and Carnatic Bank). |
| **Oldest Joint-Stock Bank** | Allahabad Bank | 1865 | Oldest joint-stock bank surviving until merger with Indian Bank in 2020. |
| **First Indian Limited Liability** | Oudh Commercial Bank | 1881 (Faizabad) | First bank with limited liability governed purely by an Indian Board of Directors (failed 1958). |
| **First Swadeshi National Bank** | Punjab National Bank (PNB) | 1894 (Commenced 1895, Lahore) | Founded by Lala Lajpat Rai and Dyal Singh Majithia; first purely Indian capital bank surviving today. |
| **Swadeshi Movement Boom** | Bank of India, Canara Bank | 1906 | Established during the anti-partition Swadeshi economic movement. |
| | Indian Bank | 1907 | Established following the collapse of Arbuthnot & Co. in Madras. |
| | Bank of Baroda | 1908 | Founded by Maharaja Sayajirao Gaekwad III. |
| **First Purely Indian Commercial** | Central Bank of India | 1911 (Sir Sorabji Pochkhanawala) | First commercial bank wholly owned and managed by Indians without foreign patronage. |
| **Imperial Amalgamation** | Imperial Bank of India | **27 January 1921** | Amalgamation of 3 Presidency Banks (Bengal, Bombay, Madras); functioned as semi-central bank. |
| **Central Bank Inception** | Reserve Bank of India (RBI) | **1 April 1935** (RBI Act, 1934) | Recommended by Hilton Young Commission (1926). Nationalised on **1 January 1949**. |
| **State Bank Genesis** | State Bank of India (SBI) | **1 July 1955** (SBI Act, 1955) | Nationalisation and transformation of Imperial Bank based on Gorwala Committee recommendations. |
| **SBI Associate Amalgamation** | 5 Associate Banks + BMB | **1 April 2017** | State Bank of Bikaner & Jaipur, Hyderabad, Mysore, Patiala, Travancore + Bharatiya Mahila Bank merged into SBI. |

### ⚖️ 3. Reserve Bank - Integrated Ombudsman Scheme (RB-IOS) Architecture

| Feature / Dimension | Statutory Parameter | Analytical Detail & Exam Trap |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Guiding Doctrine** | *"One Nation One Ombudsman"* | Integrates 3 erstwhile schemes: Banking Ombudsman (2006), NBFC Ombudsman (2018), Digital Transactions (2019). |
| **Single-Window Portal** | Centralised Receipt and Processing Centre (CRPC) | Located at **RBI Chandigarh** for centralised receipt, initial scrutiny, and digital docketing of complaints. |
| **Entities Covered** | All Commercial Banks, RRBs, Scheduled UCBs | Includes NBFCs with public deposits or asset size ≥ ₹100 Crore with customer interface; Non-bank System Participants (PPIs). |
| **Ground of Complaint** | *Deficiency in Service* | Shifted from exhaustive positive list of grounds to a broad **deficiency-based standard** (omission or commission causing distress). |
| **Compensation Ceilings** | Direct / Consequential Financial Loss | **Up to actual loss or ₹30 Lakh**, whichever is lower (exclusive of the disputed amount). |
| | Harassment, Mental Anguish & Loss of Time | **Up to ₹1 Lakh** awarded at the Ombudsman's discretion. |
| **Appellate Authority** | Executive Director, CEPD (RBI) | Either party can appeal against an award within **30 days** of receipt of the award or rejection. |
| **Ineligibility Traps** | Pre-conditions for Filing | Customer must first complain to the Regulated Entity. Can approach Ombudsman only if: (a) No reply within 30 days, or (b) Reply rejected, or (c) Complainant dissatisfied. Complaint must be filed within 1 year of RE response. |

### 📊 4. Institutional Banking Typology & Regulatory Thresholds

| Institutional Category | Statutory Foundation | Minimum Capital | Shareholding / Promoter Norms | Priority Sector Lending (PSL) Target | Mandatory Lending Restrictions |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Scheduled Commercial Banks (SCBs)** | Section 42(6) RBI Act, 1934 | ₹500 Crore (New Private Banks) | Promoter cap: max 26% of paid-up voting equity after 15 years. | **40% of ANBC** or CEOBE (whichever is higher). | Standard prudential single/group borrower caps under Large Exposures Framework (20%/25% of Tier 1). |
| **Regional Rural Banks (RRBs)** | Regional Rural Banks Act, 1976 (M. Narasimham WG) | ₹2,000 Crore authorized (₹1 Crore minimum paid-up) | **Central Govt: 50%**, **Sponsor Bank: 35%**, **State Govt: 15%**. | **75% of ANBC** | Target primarily small/marginal farmers, agricultural labourers, and rural artisans. |
| **Small Finance Banks (SFBs)** | Section 22 BR Act, 1949 (Nachiket Mor / Usha Thorat) | **₹200 Crore** (₹100 Crore for Urban Co-ops converting) | Promoter must maintain min 40% paid-up voting equity for initial 5 years; phased to 26% in 15 years. | **75% of ANBC** (40% sub-targets as per SCB formula + 35% balance to eligible PSL). | **At least 50% of loan portfolio** must comprise loans and advances of ticket size up to **₹25 Lakh**. |
| **Payments Banks** | Section 22 BR Act, 1949 (Nachiket Mor Committee) | **₹100 Crore** | Minimum 40% promoter stake for the first 5 years from commencement. | **Not Applicable (0%)** (Cannot undertake lending activities). | **Cannot lend or issue credit cards.** Maximum end-of-day balance: **₹2,00,000 per customer**. Min 75% deposits in G-Secs/T-Bills up to 1 yr. |

---88:T55e,| Parameter | Garnishee Order (Order 21 Rule 46 CPC) | Income Tax Attachment Order (Sec 226(3) IT Act) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Issuing Authority | Civil Court in execution of decree | Income Tax Officer / Tax Recovery Officer (TRO) |
| Two-Stage Process | Yes: (1) **Order Nisi** (Freeze/Show-cause) followed by (2) **Order Absolute** (Payment direction) | No: Single direct order directing immediate remittance to Govt treasury |
| Application to Future Credits | ❌ **No.** Attaches only funds existing *at the exact moment and date of service* of the order. | ✅ **Yes.** Attaches current credit balance **AND all subsequent future credits** until the full tax demand is cleared. |
| Joint Accounts Rule | Cannot attach joint account (A & B) for individual debt of A unless specified in order | Can attach proportionate share of joint holder (50% presumed unless specified) |
| Priority of Orders | Subordinate to Income Tax Attachment Orders | Enjoys **Statutory Priority** (Crown Debt) over Civil Court Garnishee Orders |
| Effect on Un-cleared Cheques | Does not attach cheques deposited but not yet cleared/credited | Attaches proceeds as soon as they clear into the account |
| Right of Set-off by Bank | Bank can exercise prior right of set-off before calculating attachable balance | Bank can exercise set-off for dues already due and payable before order date |89:T723,Under RBI Master Directions on Customer Service, banks follow structured protocols to settle claims of deceased depositors without causing undue hardship to survivors:

• **1. Accounts with Valid Nomination / Survivorship Mandate:** Claim settled in favor of the nominee or surviving joint holder within **15 days** of receipt of death certificate and KYC proof, without requiring legal representation (Probate, Letters of Administration, or Succession Certificate).
• **2. Accounts without Nomination / Survivorship (Below Threshold Limit):** Banks settle claims based on simple Indemnity Bond, Letter of Disclaimer from other legal heirs, and KYC proof up to board-approved threshold limits (typically ₹1 Lakh to ₹5 Lakhs).
• **3. Accounts with Substantial Balances & Disputes:** Legal representation (Succession Certificate under Indian Succession Act 1925) is mandatory.
• **4. Safe Deposit Lockers of Deceased:** Nominee is allowed to open the locker and inventory the contents in the presence of 2 independent witnesses; nominee receives contents as custodian for legal heirs.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Legal Orders:
1. **Future Deposits in Garnishee Order:** A deposit made in the afternoon *after* a Garnishee Order Nisi was served in the morning is **NOT** attached. The bank must open a separate new account for subsequent transactions.
2. **IT Order Priority:** If both a Garnishee Order and an IT Attachment Order arrive, the **Income Tax Attachment Order takes absolute precedence**.
3. **Deceased Account with Mandate / PoA:** A Mandate or Power of Attorney terminates **immediately** on the death, insanity, or insolvency of the principal. Any cheque signed by a PoA holder presented after the principal's death must be returned with remark 'Drawer Deceased'.

---8a:T92c,| Mortgage Type | Section in TP Act | Creation Method & Formalities | Bank's Remedy / Sale Power without Court |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Equitable Mortgage (Deposit of Title Deeds) | Section 58(f) | Delivery of original title deeds to the bank with intent to create security in notified towns. No stamp duty/registration needed in most states. | Enforceable via SARFAESI Act 2002 without court intervention; or via civil court decree for sale. |
| Simple Mortgage | Section 58(b) | Registered mortgage deed without delivery of possession. Mortgagor binds himself personally to pay the mortgage money. | Bank has no right to sell property without obtaining a Civil Court decree. |
| Mortgage by Conditional Sale | Section 58(c) | Ostensible sale of property with condition that sale becomes void on repayment, or becomes absolute on default. | Remedy is **Foreclosure** (court order debarring mortgagor from redeeming), NOT sale. |
| Usufructuary Mortgage | Section 58(d) | Delivery of possession to mortgagee who receives rents and profits in lieu of interest/principal. | Mortgagee cannot sue for sale or foreclosure; recovers debt strictly from property usufruct (rents/yields). |
| English Mortgage | Section 58(e) | Absolute transfer of property to mortgagee with agreement to re-transfer upon repayment on a fixed date. | Mortgagee has power to sell property without court intervention under Section 69 TP Act in specific conditions. |
| Anomalous Mortgage | Section 58(g) | A mortgage which is not any of the above 5 types (e.g. combination of simple and usufructuary mortgage). | Terms governed strictly by the custom and express terms in the mortgage deed. |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Collaterals & Charges:
1. **Pledge vs Hypothecation Possession:** In a Pledge, possession is **ALWAYS with the Bank**. In Hypothecation, possession is **ALWAYS with the Borrower**.
2. **Equitable Mortgage Requirements:** 3 essential requisites for a valid Equitable Mortgage under Sec 58(f): (a) a debt, (b) deposit of **ORIGINAL title deeds**, and (c) execution in a town notified by the State Government.
3. **Foreclosure Remedy:** The remedy of **Foreclosure** is available ONLY in *Mortgage by Conditional Sale* and certain *Anomalous Mortgages*. It is **NEVER** available in Simple or Equitable mortgages.

---8b:T4f1,| Section in SARFAESI | Enforcement Stage | Statutory Timelines & Powers | Borrower / Guarantor Rights |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Section 13(2) | Demand Notice to Borrower | Mandatory **60-day notice** issued to discharge liabilities in full after account is classified as NPA. | Borrower can submit representation/objection within 60 days. |
| Section 13(3A) | Disposal of Objections | Bank must reply to borrower's representation within **15 days** of receipt. | Rejection of objection gives no immediate right to appeal in DRT. |
| Section 13(4) | Enforcement / Possession Measures | Upon failure to pay after 60 days, bank takes symbolic/physical possession of secured asset, takes over management, or appoints manager. | Borrower can appeal to DRT within **45 days** under Section 17. |
| Section 14 | Magistrate Assistance | Chief Metropolitan Magistrate (CMM) or District Magistrate (DM) takes physical possession of asset and hands over to bank. | Administrative act; Magistrate cannot adjudicate dispute on merits. |
| Section 17 | Appeal to DRT (Securitisation Application) | Borrower files application against Sec 13(4) measures within 45 days. | Appeal to Appellate Tribunal (DRAT) under Sec 18 requires **50% pre-deposit** (can be reduced to 25%). |8c:T502,| Recovery Forum | Governing Act | Monetary Claim Jurisdiction | Nature of Order |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Lok Adalat | Legal Services Authorities Act, 1987 | Up to **₹20 Lakhs** (for bank compromise settlements) | Consent decree; No appeal lies against award |
| Debt Recovery Tribunal (DRT) | RDB Act, 1993 (Recovery of Debts and Bankruptcy) | **₹20 Lakhs and above** | Issues Recovery Certificate; Appeal to DRAT within 30 days |
| Insolvency & Bankruptcy Code (IBC) | Insolvency and Bankruptcy Code, 2016 (NCLT) | Default threshold of **₹1 Crore and above** | CIRP process: Resolution within 180 + 90 days (max 330 days) |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on NPA Management & SARFAESI:
1. **SARFAESI Agricultural Land Exemption:** Under Section 31(i), the SARFAESI Act does **NOT apply to agricultural land**.
2. **SARFAESI Debt Thresholds:** SARFAESI cannot be invoked if: (a) remaining debt is less than **₹1 Lakh**, or (b) remaining principal and interest is less than **20% of the original principal debt**.
3. **DRAT Pre-Deposit Requirement:** To appeal before the Debt Recovery Appellate Tribunal (DRAT) under Section 18 of SARFAESI, the borrower must deposit **50% of the debt determined by DRT** (DRAT can reduce it to minimum 25%).

---8d:T64d,$\text{PV} = \frac{\text{FV}}{(1 + r)^n} = \text{FV} \times (1 + r)^{-n}$

*Explanation:* Where \(PV\) = Present Value, \(FV\) = Future Value, \(r\) = interest rate per compounding period, and \(n\) = total number of compounding periods.

$\text{PV}_{\text{Annuity}} = C \times \left[ \frac{1 - (1 + r)^{-n}}{r} \right]$

*Explanation:* Used to determine the maximum loan amount sanctionable for a given equated periodic installment \(C\).

$\text{PV}_{\text{Perpetuity}} = \frac{C}{r}$

*Explanation:* A perpetuity is a stream of equal cash flows \(C\) that continues indefinitely forever (e.g. British Consols, irredeemable preference shares).

$\text{PV}_{\text{Growing Perpetuity}} = \frac{C}{r - g} \quad (r > g)$

*Explanation:* Where cash flows grow at constant annual rate \(g\) indefinitely (provided discount rate \(r > g\)).

$\text{EAR} = \left( 1 + \frac{r_{\text{nominal}}}{m} \right)^m - 1$

*Explanation:* Reflects true annual yield when interest is compounded \(m\) times per year (e.g. quarterly compounding in bank deposits where \(m=4\)).

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on TVM & Annuity:
1. **Annuity Due vs Ordinary Annuity:** \(\text{Value of Annuity Due} = \text{Value of Ordinary Annuity} \times (1 + r)\). Annuity Due is always higher by a factor of \((1+r)\) because payments earn interest for one extra period.
2. **Nominal Rate vs Effective Rate:** If nominal rate is 12% p.a. compounded monthly, \(\text{EAR} = (1 + 0.01)^{12} - 1 = 12.68\%\), which is higher than 12%.
3. **Perpetuity Formula:** Present value of perpetuity is simply \(\frac{C}{r}\).

---8e:T800,| Ratio Category | Formula | Banking Benchmark / Target | Significance for Lenders |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Current Ratio (CR) | \(\frac{\text{Current Assets (CA)}}{\text{Current Liabilities (CL)}}\) | **1.33 : 1** (Tandon Method 2) | Measures short-term solvency and working capital cushion. |
| Quick / Acid-Test Ratio | \(\frac{\text{Current Assets} - \text{Inventories} - \text{Prepaid Expenses}}{\text{Current Liabilities}}\) | **1.00 : 1** | Instant liquidity test excluding slow-moving inventory. |
| Debt-Equity Ratio (DER) | \(\frac{\text{Total Long-Term Debt}}{\text{Tangible Net Worth (Net Owned Funds)}}\) | **2 : 1** (or lower; 3:1 for infra projects) | Measures capital structure leverage and financial cushion of equity holders. |
| Interest Coverage Ratio (ICR) | \(\frac{\text{EBIT (Earnings Before Interest & Tax)}}{\text{Interest Expense}}\) | **\ge 2.00\text{ times}** | Measures ability to pay annual interest obligations from operating profits. |
| Debt Service Coverage Ratio (DSCR) | \(\frac{\text{PAT} + \text{Depreciation} + \text{Interest}}{\text{Interest} + \text{Principal Installment}}\) | **1.50 to 2.00** (Min 1.20) | Primary benchmark for sanctioning term loans. |
| Inventory Turnover Ratio (ITR) | \(\frac{\text{Cost of Goods Sold (COGS)}}{\text{Average Inventory}}\) | Higher indicates fast-moving stock | Detects inventory hoarding or obsolete stock build-up. |
| Debtors Collection Period | \(\frac{\text{Average Accounts Receivable}}{\text{Net Credit Sales}} \times 365\) | Within credit terms (e.g. 60–90 days) | Measures speed of realizing cash from credit customers. |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Ratio Analysis:
1. **Quick Ratio Exclusion:** When calculating Quick Assets, **Inventories and Prepaid Expenses are SUBTRACTED** from Current Assets.
2. **Tandon Committee Benchmark:** The standard minimum Current Ratio under Tandon Method 2 is **1.33:1**.
3. **Ideal Debt-Equity Ratio:** Standard acceptable commercial benchmark for Debt-Equity Ratio is **2:1**.

---

---8f:T49f,$\text{P/V Ratio} = \frac{\text{Contribution}}{\text{Sales}} \times 100 = \frac{\text{Sales} - \text{Variable Cost}}{\text{Sales}} \times 100$

*Explanation:* Expresses profitability per unit of sales volume. Higher P/V ratio indicates high profit-earning capacity.

$\text{BEP (Units)} = \frac{\text{Fixed Cost}}{\text{Contribution per Unit}}, \quad \text{BEP (Value)} = \frac{\text{Fixed Cost}}{\text{P/V Ratio}}$

*Explanation:* The sales volume at which Total Revenue equals Total Cost (Profit = 0).

$\text{Margin of Safety (MoS)} = \text{Actual Sales} - \text{Break-Even Sales} = \frac{\text{Profit}}{\text{P/V Ratio}}$

*Explanation:* The safety cushion by which sales can decline before the business begins incurring losses.

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Marginal Costing:
1. **Fixed Cost per Unit Trap:** Total Fixed Cost remains constant, but **Fixed Cost per unit DECREASES as production volume increases**.
2. **Variable Cost per Unit Trap:** Variable cost per unit remains **CONSTANT**, but total variable cost increases proportionally with volume.
3. **Margin of Safety Formula:** \(\text{MOS} = \frac{\text{Profit}}{\text{P/V Ratio}}\).

---90:T5e6,| Product | Loan / Credit Slabs | Margin Requirement | Security / Collateral Mandates |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Education Loan (Studies in India / Abroad) | Up to **₹4.00 Lakhs** | **NIL (0% Margin)** | Co-obligation of parents. **NO third-party guarantee or collateral security** can be demanded. |
| Education Loan | Above **₹4.00 Lakhs up to ₹7.50 Lakhs** | 5% for Studies in India; 15% for Studies Abroad | Co-obligation of parents + Suitable Third-Party Guarantee (or CGFSEL guarantee cover). |
| Education Loan | Above **₹7.50 Lakhs** | 5% for Studies in India; 15% for Studies Abroad | Co-obligation of parents + **Tangible Collateral Security** of suitable value. |
| Credit Card - Minimum Amount Due (MAD) | Typically **5%** of total outstanding balance | N/A | Grace period of 20–50 days (interest-free credit). If full bill not paid, interest charged on entire balance from transaction date. |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Retail Products:
1. **Housing Loan LTV Caps:** Maximum LTV for loans up to ₹30L is **90%**; for loans > ₹75L it is **75%**.
2. **Collateral for Education Loans:** For education loans up to **₹4 Lakhs**, demanding collateral security or third-party guarantee is a direct violation of RBI/IBA guidelines.
3. **External Benchmark Lending Rate (EBLR):** Since October 1, 2019, all new floating rate retail loans (housing, auto, personal) must be mandatorily linked to an **External Benchmark** (e.g. RBI Policy Repo Rate).

---91:T56e,| Dimension | Mandatory Regulatory Guideline / Standard | Legal Authority / Reference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| DRA Certification | All recovery agents must undergo 50/100-hour training and hold mandatory **IIBF DRA Certification** | RBI Master Directions on Recovery Agents |
| Permitted Contact Hours | Calls and visits permitted **ONLY between 07:00 AM and 07:00 PM** (07:00 to 19:00 hrs) | RBI Fair Practices Code |
| Privacy & Conduct Standards | Strict prohibition on abusive language, intimidation, public shaming, or contacting debtor's friends/workplace | Section 35A BR Act & Consumer Protection Act 2019 |
| Notice before Visit | Borrower must be given identity of agent and authorization letter before recovery visit | IBA Code of Conduct |
| Grievance Redressal | Bank must maintain dedicated grievance mechanism for recovery complaints; bank is vicariously liable for agent misconduct | Supreme Court in ICICI Bank v. Prakash Kaur (2007) |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps on Credit Scoring & Recovery:
1. **DRA Calling Hours:** Recovery calls can be made strictly between **07:00 AM and 07:00 PM** (not 08:00 AM to 08:00 PM).
2. **CIBIL Score Range:** CIBIL scores range from **300 to 900 points**.
3. **CIBIL Score Factor Weightages:** **Past Payment History (35%)** has the highest individual weightage in determining a borrower's credit score.

---

---92:T12f6,> 🧠 **Key Concept — IIBF Core Foundation: Retail Prudential Norms & Wealth Laws**
> Retail banking requires strict compliance with RBI prudential Loan-to-Value (LTV) limits, IBA model education financing norms, service marketing delivery, and RERA buyer protections.

### 🏠 1. Regulatory Loan-to-Value (LTV) Ratios for Housing Loans

*To prevent systemic asset bubbles, the RBI mandates loan ceilings linked to property market valuations:*

| Individual Housing Loan Category | Maximum Permissible LTV Ratio | Minimum Borrower Margin |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Up to ₹30 Lakh** | **90%** | **10%** |
| **Above ₹30 Lakh to ₹75 Lakh** | **80%** | **20%** |
| **Above ₹75 Lakh** | **75%** | **25%** |

• *LTV Calculation Rule:* For loans above ₹10 Lakh, stamp duty, registration charges, and documentation expenses **cannot be included** in the total property cost for calculating the LTV ratio.

### 🎓 2. IBA Model Education Loan Scheme Norms

| Loan Quantum Slab | Margin Requirement | Collateral & Security Mandate |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Up to ₹4.00 Lakh** | **Nil (0% Margin)** | **No Collateral, No Third-Party Guarantee**. Parents/guardians as co-borrowers. |
| **₹4.00 Lakh to ₹7.50 Lakh** | • **5%** for studies in India<br>• **15%** for studies abroad | Third-party guarantee acceptable to bank; **No tangible collateral**. |
| **Above ₹7.50 Lakh** | • **5%** for studies in India<br>• **15%** for studies abroad | **Tangible Collateral Security** of full value + co-obligation of parents. |

• **Moratorium / Repayment Holiday:** Course duration + **1 year** (or 6 months after securing employment, whichever is earlier).
• **Interest Rate Concession:** 1% interest concession if simple interest is serviced during the study and moratorium period.

### 📢 3. Extended Marketing Mix for Banking Services (The 7 Ps)

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                 THE 7 Ps OF FINANCIAL SERVICES MARKETING                    │
├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
│ 1. PRODUCT           : Savings deposits, personal loans, credit cards.      │
│ 2. PRICE             : Interest rate spread, processing fees, penal charges.│
│ 3. PLACE             : Physical branches, ATMs, mobile banking apps, BCs.   │
│ 4. PROMOTION         : Digital marketing, branding, financial literacy.     │
│ 5. PEOPLE            : Branch staff, tellers, certified relationship mgrs.  │
│ 6. PROCESS           : Instant digital onboarding, e-KYC, turnaround times. │
│ 7. PHYSICAL EVIDENCE : Modern branch architecture, secure UI/UX, passbooks. │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

• **Cross-Selling vs. Up-Selling in Retail Banking:**
  - *Cross-Selling:* Selling an ancillary, complementary financial product to an existing customer (e.g. selling motor insurance or mutual fund SIP to a home loan borrower).
  - *Up-Selling:* Persuading a customer to upgrade to a higher-tier product (e.g. upgrading a savings account to a premium wealth account with higher minimum balance).

### 🏢 4. Real Estate Regulation Act (RERA, 2016) Safeguards

• **Mandatory Project Registration:** Every residential or commercial project with land area exceeding **500 square meters** or containing more than **8 apartments** must be registered with State RERA prior to public advertising or sale.
• **Mandatory 70% Escrow Account:** Promoters must deposit **70% of the funds realized from allottees into a dedicated scheduled bank escrow account** to cover land and construction costs. Funds can only be withdrawn based on percentage completion certified by an engineer, architect, and chartered accountant.
• **Structural Defect Guarantee:** Promoter must rectify any structural defects or poor workmanship within **5 years** of handover without charging the buyer.

> 🎯 **Top IIBF Traps for Unit 21:**
> 1. **LTV Cap on Large Loans:** Loans above ₹75 Lakh have a maximum LTV of **75%** (minimum 25% margin).
> 2. **Education Loan Collateral:** Collateral cannot be demanded for loans **up to ₹7.50 Lakh** under the IBA model scheme.
> 3. **RERA Escrow Proportion:** Exactly **70% of buyer collections** must be locked into the dedicated project escrow account.

---93:T644,| Exam Question / Concept | Correct Statutory Answer | Key Regulatory Citation / Logic |
| --- | --- | --- |
| What is the total Priority Sector Lending target for Small Finance Banks and RRBs? | **75% of ANBC / CEOBE** | RBI Master Directions on Priority Sector Lending (compared to 40% for domestic commercial banks). |
| Which year is celebrated as the 'Year of Great Divide' in Indian demographic history? | **1921** | Before 1921 population growth was stagnant/negative; post-1921 population exhibited continuous explosive growth. |
| Under WTO Agreement on Agriculture, what is the de-minimis amber box support limit for developing countries like India? | **10% of total agricultural production value** | Developed countries have a 5% limit; developing nations are permitted 10%. |
| Under the revised MSME criteria (July 2020), what is the turnover limit for a Small Enterprise? | **₹50 Crores** (Investment limit is ₹10 Crores) | Composite criteria: Micro (₹1Cr/₹5Cr), Small (₹10Cr/₹50Cr), Medium (₹50Cr/₹250Cr). |
| Sovereign Gold Bonds (SGBs) carry an annual coupon interest rate of? | **2.50% p.a. payable semi-annually** | 8-year tenor with exit option from 5th year; capital gains exempt on redemption for individuals. |

> 🎯 **Exam Anchor & Trap:**
> 🎯 Top Exam Traps in Paper 1:
1. **CCB Component:** Minimum CET1 is 5.50%. When Capital Conservation Buffer (2.50%) is added, the total CET1 requirement becomes **8.00%**.
2. **MSME Turnover Calculation:** Export turnover is **EXCLUDED** from the calculation of total annual turnover for MSME classification.

1. **DRA Calling Hours:** Recovery calls are permitted strictly between **07:00 AM and 07:00 PM**.
2. **Education Loan Margin:** 0% margin for loans up to **₹4 Lakhs**.
3. **RERA Escrow Percentage:** It is **70%**, NOT 50% or 100%.
